# AI Run Log Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** Give every conversation-fact-extraction run a durable, queryable record - the input window, the model's raw response, every finding and what we did with it, and the human verdict that followed - readable through an admin-only two-pane log at `/settings/ai-runs`.

**Architecture:** A new single-key `ai_runs` DynamoDB table holds two disjoint row kinds (`run#<runId>` full records, `ptr#<entityKey>#<startedAt>#<runId>` adjacency pointers) behind one sparse `byEntity` GSI, all TTL'd at 90 days. `processRow` in `app/src/jobs/extraction.ts` stops throwing and instead fills a `RunDraft` its CALLER owns, returning an outcome; `runDueExtractions` performs the single best-effort write. Verdicts are stamped later by three resolution surfaces via targeted nested-path `UpdateItem`s guarded with `attribute_exists(itemId)`.

**Tech Stack:** TypeScript (NodeNext ESM), Express, DynamoDB via `@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb`, Vitest, React 19 + react-router-dom, Playwright, Terraform.

**Authoritative spec (frozen; do NOT redesign):** `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-08-06-ai-run-log-design.md`, at its CURRENT revision. Five passages are marked `AMENDED post-approval` (spec lines 172, 344, 520, 715, and the surface-1 enumeration in 7.3); this plan implements the amended text. Section references below (`spec 6.1`, `spec 7.2`, ...) point at it. If you believe the spec is wrong, STOP and raise it - do not deviate silently.

---

## Global Constraints

- **ASCII only** on every line you add. Verify with `tr -d '\11\12\15\40-\176' < FILE | wc -c` -> must print `0`. `app/src/lib/tables.ts` is a PRE-EXISTING non-ASCII file; only your ADDED lines must be ASCII there.
- **Never rewrite a source file with PowerShell** `Get-Content`/`-replace`/`Set-Content` (BOM-less UTF-8 read as ANSI produces mojibake). Use the Edit tool.
- **Gates, run BARE, never piped** (redirect to a file, then grep the file):
  - `npm run typecheck` - REQUIRED SEPARATE GATE every slice. `npm test` and e2e run through esbuild/tsx, which strip types WITHOUT checking them.
  - `npm test` - all workspaces.
  - `timeout 1500 npm run e2e` - the outer cap is mandatory; run ONLY from the worktree root. `npm run e2e -- --flag` never reaches playwright (npm eats it).
- **Commit discipline:** a gating bare `git status` READ as its OWN command before EVERY commit (check for `.git/MERGE_HEAD` and others' staged state); stage EXPLICIT paths only, never `git add -A`; use `git commit -- <paths> -m "..."`. **Every commit message ends with this trailer** (stated once here; each task's commit step gives only the subject line):
  `Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 (1M context) <noreply@anthropic.com>`
- **No infra, ever, without an explicit human ask:** no `terraform plan/apply`, no `secrets:push`, no SSM writes, no `deploy:*`, no real `.env.*` edits. `npm run gen:tables` is NOT infra - it is a code generator, and it IS a build task here (spec 12).
- **PII rule, binding on all new code:** NEVER log message bodies, phone numbers, `rawText`, `rawResult`, or any decision value. Log ids, field names, counts, and reasons only. This is why `processRow` fills a caller-owned draft instead of throwing one (spec 8).
- **New app runtime deps go in `app/package.json`**, never the root. This feature needs NO new dependency - `node:crypto` is built in.
- Worktree is `w:/tmp/ai-run-log` on branch `feat/ai-run-log`. Never move HEAD in the shared main checkout.

## The failure mode this plan is most prone to: tests that cannot fail

Three separate defects in review round 2 were the same species, and one in round 1 was too: **a test whose named code path is unreachable from the fixture it uses.** It reports success, ends the investigation, and is strictly worse than no test. Before adding or editing any test in this plan, ask the two questions:

1. **What would have to break for this to go red?** If the answer is "nothing I can name", the test is decoration.
2. **Is the path it names actually reachable from this fixture?** Check the gates between the fixture and the assertion.

The four instances already caught, kept here so they are not reintroduced:

- **The fake driver's `rawText`** carrying the `EXTRACT:` prefix would make `parseExtractionOps` return the empty view, so every decision records `not_addressed` and every e2e decision assertion passes vacuously. Closed by an exact-string test plus `not.toContain('EXTRACT:')` (Task 9).
- **The `runDueExtractions` backstop** is unreachable from any site `processRow` wraps - which is all six repo/`complete` sites. Closed by throwing from `applyEvents.emit`, which is unwrapped (Task 20).
- **A `no_new_client` skip** cannot be produced by a second `extractionTick`: `claim()` removes the due keys, so there are zero due rows and no run at all (Task 30).
- **An `ineligible_type` skip** cannot be produced by a landlord inbound: `app/src/routes/webhooks/twilio.ts:1158-1161` arms a due row only for `tenant_1to1` / `unknown_1to1`, so the fixture would produce zero runs (Task 30).

Two more, caught in the round that audited the sweep itself:

- **A LABEL is not a VALUE.** `getByText(/extraction driver/i)` matches static chrome and passes whether or not the flag is plumbed through - a replacement assertion that was itself unfalsifiable. Assert `getByLabel('Extraction driver: fake')`; Task 29 pins the `` `${label}: ${value}` `` accessible name that makes it possible.
- **"Unproducible" is a claim about the code, and must be verified like one.** `noContent` was declared unreachable by reading only the first disjunct of `toUtterances`' guard (`app/src/jobs/extraction.ts:131`); the second, `!m.transcript`, is satisfied by an empty-string transcript and the whole chain is reachable from the existing dev seam. A false impossibility claim quietly abandons real coverage AND is believed, because nobody re-checks it.

Three general rules that follow, applied throughout:

- **Guard the guard.** A `not.toContain` assertion over captured logs passes when the capture is empty. Assert the capture is non-empty and contains a known line first (Task 20's PII guard).
- **Assert the precondition, in the test.** Where a fixture must be in a particular state for the path to be reachable, assert that state before the payload assertion, so fixture drift fails RED at the precondition rather than passing silently (Task 30's `pending` check, Task 19's `expect(h.seen).toHaveLength(0)`).
- **Every claim of the form "impossible / unreachable / unproducible / already true" carries a file:line, and is re-checked before the plan ships.** Those claims are load-bearing precisely because they end the investigation. The surviving ones are audited in Self-Review item 6.

## Honest scope note: which steps are code and which are specification

Read this before executing. Every step in this plan is one of two things, and each is labelled:

- **CODE steps** carry the literal text to write. Everything in Slices 1-14 and 16 is a CODE step.
- **SPECIFICATION steps** give an exact contract (props, DOM roles, states, required tests) but not the literal JSX. There are exactly **three**, all in Slice 15: Task 28 Step 4 (the two data hooks) and Task 29 Steps 5 and 1 (the three React components and their test bodies). They are ordinary React over a fully specified API, the repo has four structurally identical precedents named inline, and writing the JSX here would add length without adding a decision. **Nothing outside Slice 15 is sketched.**

---

## Slice Order and Coherence

The tree must typecheck and pass `npm test` at EVERY slice boundary. The three breaking interface changes are each contained in ONE slice with all of their fallout.

| Slice | What lands |
| --- | --- |
| 1 | `ai_runs` table registration + regenerated tfvars |
| 2 | `runTypes.ts` vocabulary; `aiRunsRepo` (atomic `putRun`, `listByEntity`, `setVerdict`) |
| 3 | **BREAKING 1** required `TranscriptUtterance.tsMsgId` + exported `renderUtteranceLine` |
| 4 | **BREAKING 2** `ExtractionDriver.extract` -> `ExtractionCall` (with a temporary unwrap at the one job call site) |
| 5 | **BREAKING 3** `putSuggestion` returns the displaced row; `runId` on suggestions |
| 6 | `ApplyOutcome` per-target decisions + drop reasons (purely additive) |
| 7 | `parseExtractionOps` (TOTAL) in `schema.ts` |
| 8 | `runWindow.ts` (light + full) + export the four job helpers |
| 9 | `decisions.ts` |
| 10 | `processRow` fills a caller-owned `RunDraft`; single write; all three `ExtractionJobDeps` sites |
| 11 | Verdict write-back on all three resolution surfaces |
| 12 | `messagesRepo` point-get + batch-get |
| 13 | `SystemFlags` extraction fields (server + dashboard mirror + `FlagPills`) |
| 14 | Admin API, mounted |
| 15 | Dashboard `/settings/ai-runs` |
| 16 | e2e, docs, `main` sync, final gates |

**Named coherence gaps.** Each is a feature gap, never a broken tree, and none is user-visible because the surface does not exist until Slice 15.

1. **Slices 4 through 9:** `extract` returns a discriminated union but the job immediately re-throws on `ok:false`, so spec 6.4's benefit ("`rawText` survives a parse failure") is not yet realized - there is no envelope writer. Job behavior (backoff, park, attempt counts) is deliberately UNCHANGED across this window; **Task 11's regression test** pins it.
2. **Slices 5 through 9:** `SuggestionItem.runId` exists and `putSuggestion` reports displaced rows, but the JOB does not pass `ctx.runId` until Slice 10. Any suggestion written by a live run in this window carries NO `runId` and is therefore permanently unstampable by all three verdict surfaces. Harmless in a worktree (nothing runs live), but **do not deploy this branch mid-slice**. Slices 5-9 are five commits on one working day; if that is not acceptable, hoist Task 18's `ctx.runId` plumbing into Slice 5 - it is a two-line change and the only reason it sits in Slice 10 is that `RunDraft.runId` is minted there.
3. **Slices 5 through 10:** nobody stamps `superseded` on a displaced earlier run.
4. **Slices 10 and 11:** run records exist and carry `verdict: 'pending'` on suggested decisions, but no resolution surface writes back, so every pending decision stays `pending` regardless of what a human does. Closed by Slice 11.
5. **Slices 13 and 14:** `SystemFlags` carries the four new fields and `FlagPills` renders them on `/settings/system`; the run-log page whose header spec 9 wants them in does not exist until Slice 15. `FlagPills` is the single renderer either way.

- [ ] **Step 3: Register the table (ASCII only on these added lines)**

```ts
  {
    // NEW in ai-run-log (README deviation): the durable record of every
    // conversation-fact-extraction run. Two disjoint row kinds share the key:
    //   run#<runId>                          the full run record
    //   ptr#<entityKey>#<startedAt>#<runId>  an adjacency pointer
    // byEntity is TRULY sparse - only ptr# rows carry entityKey/sortKey, so a
    // run# row never appears in the index. entityKey is either a LITERAL SCOPE
    // TOKEN ('global', 'outcome#<outcome>') or the `<table>#<id>` convention
    // audit_events uses ('conversations#<id>', 'contacts#<id>'); both forms
    // coexist here by design. sortKey is `<startedAt ISO>#<runId>`, so a scope
    // reads newest-first with ScanIndexForward false and a date range is a
    // BETWEEN. Reads are Query(byEntity) then ONE BatchGetItem - never a Scan.
    //
    // Why a separate table (design 5.1): adding run# rows to ai_extraction
    // would force table-wide TTL onto a table holding PERMANENT dismissal
    // tombstones and extraction cursors, which survive only by lacking
    // expires_at.
    //
    // TTL: 90 days on BOTH row kinds. DynamoDB TTL is asynchronous and may lag
    // up to 48h, unordered, so a pointer can outlive its run# row - readers
    // render that entry as expired rather than erroring.
    baseName: 'ai_runs',
    hashKey: { name: 'itemId', type: 'S' },
    gsis: [
      {
        indexName: 'byEntity',
        hashKey: { name: 'entityKey', type: 'S' },
        rangeKey: { name: 'sortKey', type: 'S' },
        sparse: true,
      },
    ],
    ttlAttribute: 'expires_at',
  },
```

Run: `npm run gen:tables`
Expected: `wrote  infra/envs/dev/tables.auto.tfvars.json (21 tables)` and the same for prod. Inspect `git diff infra/` - the ONLY change must be a new `"ai_runs"` block inserted alphabetically after `"activity_events"`.

- Produces: `DECISION_TARGETS`, `DecisionTarget`, `isDecisionTarget`, `ProposedOp`, `DecisionOutcome`, `DROP_REASONS`, `DropReason`, `Verdict`, `RunDecision`, `RunWindowMessage`, `RunWindowExcluded`, `RunWindowParams`, `RunWindow`. Consumed by Tasks 3, 6-9, 12, 14, 17, 19-23, 26.

```ts
    expect(DECISION_TARGETS).toEqual([
      'firstName', 'lastName', 'voucherSize', 'housingAuthority',

  });

      'invalid_value',

      'phone_not_canonicalizable',
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the module**

```ts
// Shared vocabulary for the AI run log (design 2026-08-06, sections 6-7).
//
// LEAF MODULE BY DESIGN: this file imports NOTHING. apply.ts describes its
// per-target outcomes with these types and the ai_runs repo stores them, but
// apply.ts never imports the repo - so the apply layer gains no ai_runs
// dependency. That separation is exactly why the `superseded` stamp is routed
// through the JOB rather than through apply (design 7.3/8).

/** The twelve targets a run can decide about. Order is the display order. */
export const DECISION_TARGETS = [
  'firstName', 'lastName', 'voucherSize', 'housingAuthority',
  'pets', 'evictions', 'tenure', 'porting',
  'address', 'status', 'type', 'phone',
] as const;

export type DecisionTarget = (typeof DECISION_TARGETS)[number];

/** True when `value` is one of the twelve targets (route/param validation). */
export function isDecisionTarget(value: unknown): value is DecisionTarget {
  return typeof value === 'string' && (DECISION_TARGETS as readonly string[]).includes(value);
}

/**
 * What the MODEL said for a target, read from rawText BEFORE any folding or
 * downgrading (design 7.1). `none` is an explicit decline; `absent` means the
 * target never appeared. Conflating them would manufacture evidence of
 * evaluation that never happened.
 */
export type ProposedOp = 'write' | 'suggest' | 'none' | 'absent';

/** What WE did with the target. */
export type DecisionOutcome = 'wrote' | 'suggested' | 'dropped' | 'no_finding' | 'not_addressed';

/**
 * Every discard branch a run can record (design 7.2, as amended).
 *
 * `empty_value_at_parse` is DISTINCT from `invalid_value` and the difference is
 * load-bearing: the first is the model sending nothing usable (an empty or
 * whitespace value that schema.ts:215-219, or the zero-usable-parts address
 * fold at schema.ts:258-263, erases before apply ever runs); the second is
 * apply's coerceField rejecting a well-formed but out-of-range value
 * (apply.ts:175). QA needs to tell those apart.
 *
 * Deliberately EXCLUDED: `note_line_filtered` (apply.ts:409-411 - note lines
 * are not decision targets, so it would be an orphan key) and
 * `address_no_usable_parts` (apply.ts:226 - unreachable; schema.ts:258-263
 * already drops a zero-part address before apply is called). `lossy_address`
 * and `demoted_inferred_role` are NOT drop reasons either: both produce
 * SUGGESTIONS (apply.ts:256-284 and :189-211) and are recorded as outcome
 * 'suggested' with demotedFrom 'write'.
 */
export const DROP_REASONS = [
  'wrong_contact_type', // apply.ts:169, :224
  'invalid_value', // apply.ts:175
  'equal_to_current', // apply.ts:198, :270
  'status_not_onboarding_tenant', // apply.ts:338
  'type_already_classified', // apply.ts:360
  'phone_not_canonicalizable', // apply.ts:367
  'phone_already_owned', // apply.ts:369
  'phone_owned_by_other', // apply.ts:379
  'dismissed_before', // apply.ts putSuggestionSafe
  'repo_error', // apply.ts putSuggestionSafe
  'empty_value_at_parse', // never reaches apply - schema.ts folded it away
] as const;

export type DropReason = (typeof DROP_REASONS)[number];

/**
 * TOTAL - never blank, because blank is indistinguishable from "nobody has
 * looked yet" (design 7.3).
 *   wrote                    -> auto_applied
 *   suggested                -> pending -> accepted | dismissed | superseded |
 *                               superseded_by_human_edit
 *   dropped                  -> not_presented
 *   no_finding/not_addressed -> not_presented
 */
export type Verdict =
  | 'auto_applied'
  | 'pending'
  | 'accepted'
  | 'dismissed'
  | 'superseded'
  | 'superseded_by_human_edit'
  | 'not_presented';

/** One target's full story: what the model proposed, what we did, what a human decided. */
export interface RunDecision {
  proposedOp: ProposedOp;
  /** The RAW value from rawText (pre-coercion). PII by design (6.2). */
  proposedValue?: string;
  /** The apply-layer coerced value actually written/suggested. PII by design. */
  coercedValue?: unknown;
  /** What the field held BEFORE - the single field that makes "changed from
   *  what?" answerable, deliberately kept (6.2). PII by design. */
  previousValue?: string;
  reason?: string;
  /** Set to 'write' when a write was ROUTED to the suggest path (a Layer-3
   *  inferred-role demotion, or a lossy address write). Not a drop. */
  demotedFrom?: 'write';
  outcome: DecisionOutcome;
  /**
   * ABSENT on a `dropped` outcome means the join found no explanation - a plan
   * or apply GAP, never a normal state. buildDecisions logs a warn when it
   * produces one, and a reasonless drop renders as "unexplained" in the UI.
   */
  dropReason?: DropReason;
  verdict: Verdict;
  verdictAt?: string;
  verdictBy?: string;
}

/**
 * One message in the window. The four byte fields are present ONLY on a `full`
 * window: a SKIP records a LIGHT window (design 6.3, as amended), because
 * capping and hashing happen after the freshness gate and `no_new_client` is
 * the common steady-state outcome - doing that work purely to log it would put
 * it on the hot path. Bytes only matter when bytes were sent.
 */
export interface RunWindowMessage {
  tsMsgId: string;
  /** MessageItem.type at run time (sms | mms | call | email). */
  type: string;
  /** MessageItem.direction at run time (inbound | outbound). */
  direction: string;
  /** 'new' = newer than the cursor (full-fidelity read); 'seen' = context. */
  tier: 'new' | 'seen';
  /** FULL only. The EXACT per-message cap this run applied - not just the tier
   *  label, so a later tuning of NEW_/SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP cannot
   *  misrepresent historical runs. */
  capChars?: number;
  /** FULL only. */
  truncated?: boolean;
  /** FULL only. Total utterance length AFTER capping. */
  chars?: number;
  /** FULL only. sha256, first 16 hex, of the EXACT rendered text this message
   *  contributed to the user content - produced by the SAME renderer that
   *  builds the request (6.1). Drift detection, not a replay guarantee. */
  hash?: string;
}

/** A message that was fetched but never reached the model, with its cause. */
export interface RunWindowExcluded {
  tsMsgId: string;
  /** age_30d: older than MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS (jobs/extraction.ts, the
   *  cutoff filter in processRow). char_budget: pushed out by
   *  WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET - FULL windows only, since a light window never runs
   *  the budget fill. */
  cause: 'age_30d' | 'char_budget';
}

/** The byte-affecting CONSTANTS this run used (design 4, 6.1). FULL only:
 *  recording them lets a reader tell a constant change from an algorithm
 *  change - if the params match and a hash does not, the CODE changed. */
export interface RunWindowParams {
  newMessageCharCap: number;
  seenMessageCharCap: number;
  windowCharBudget: number;
  maxTranscriptMessages: number;
  maxTranscriptAgeDays: number;
  truncationMarker: string;
}

export interface RunWindow {
  /**
   * 'light' - which messages existed and where the cursor sat. Recorded on a
   * SKIP. No capping, no chars, no hashes, no char_budget exclusions.
   * 'full'  - everything, recorded once the model was actually going to be
   * called.
   */
  detail: 'light' | 'full';
  cursor: string;
  newestTsMsgId?: string;
  /** FULL only. */
  hasInferredRoleContent?: boolean;
  /** FULL only. */
  totalChars?: number;
  /** True when the fetch returned exactly MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES rows, so
   *  OLDER messages may exist unseen. They are never fetched, so they cannot be
   *  enumerated in `excluded` without a second read this design declines to pay
   *  for - an honest boolean beats implying the list is exhaustive (6.1). */
  windowCappedAtLimit: boolean;
  /** FULL only. */
  windowParams?: RunWindowParams;
  messages: RunWindowMessage[];
  excluded: RunWindowExcluded[];
  /** FULL only. Messages that produced ZERO utterances and therefore carried
   *  NOTHING to the model - in practice a call whose transcript is not
   *  `completed`. Such a message still enters the included set with size 0;
   *  recording it as READ would be a false claim that the model saw a call it
   *  never saw (6.1). */
  noContent?: string[];
}
```

### Task 3: `aiRunsRepo` - types and an ATOMIC `putRun`

**Files:** Create `app/src/repos/aiRunsRepo.ts`, `app/test/aiRunsRepo.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `RepoDeps` (`app/src/repos/conversationsRepo.ts:341-347`), `tableName` (`app/src/lib/config.ts`), `getDocumentClient` (`app/src/lib/dynamo.ts`), Task 2's vocabulary.
- Produces: `RUN_TTL_DAYS`, `DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE`, `runItemId`, `runSortKey`, `pointerItemId`, `pointerEntityKeys`, `runExpiresAt`; `RunTrigger`, `RunOutcome`, `SkipReason`, `RunErrorKind`, `RunError`, `RunUsage`, `AiRunRecord`, `AiRunRecordInput`, `AiRunPointer`, `AiRunListEntry`, `AiRunsRepo`, `createAiRunsRepo`. Consumed by Tasks 18-23, 26, 27.

**Atomicity is a requirement, not a nicety.** The run row and its pointers go in ONE `TransactWriteItems`. Five items is far under the 100-item / 4 MB transaction limits, and a partial fan-out would permanently hide a run from some scopes while showing it in others - a silent, unrepairable inconsistency in the very artifact this feature exists to make trustworthy.

3. Add a `TransactWriteCommand` branch. It must be ALL-OR-NOTHING and support an injected failure so atomicity is testable:

```ts
      if (cmd instanceof TransactWriteCommand) {
        const items = (cmd.input.TransactItems ?? []) as Array<{ Put?: { Item: Row } }>;
        if (failTransactAfter !== undefined && items.length > failTransactAfter) {
          // Model a rejected transaction: NOTHING is written.
          throw new TransactionCanceledException({ message: 'cancelled', $metadata: {} });
        }
        for (const it of items) {
          const item = it.Put?.Item;
          if (item === undefined) throw new Error('fake doc: only Put is modelled in transactions');
          const stored: Row = {};
          for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(item)) if (v !== undefined) stored[k] = v;
          store.set(item['itemId'] as string, stored);
        }
        return {};
      }
```

4. Add a `BatchGetCommand` branch with an injectable throttle (needed by Task 4). It deliberately returns items REVERSED, so a reader that trusts response order fails:

```ts
      if (cmd instanceof BatchGetCommand) {
        batchGetCalls += 1;
        const table = Object.keys(cmd.input.RequestItems ?? {})[0]!;
        const req = cmd.input.RequestItems![table] as { Keys?: Array<{ itemId: string }> };
        const keys = req.Keys ?? [];
        const withhold = batchGetCalls <= throttleFirstN ? keys.slice(Math.ceil(keys.length / 2)) : [];
        const served = keys.filter((k) => !withhold.some((w) => w.itemId === k.itemId));
        const items = served
          .map((k) => store.get(k.itemId))
          .filter((r): r is Row => r !== undefined)
          .map((r) => ({ ...r }))
          .reverse(); // BatchGetItem is UNORDERED - model that (design 5.3)
        return {
          Responses: { [table]: items },
          ...(withhold.length > 0 && { UnprocessedKeys: { [table]: { Keys: withhold } } }),
        };
      }
```

Signature becomes `makeFakeDoc(opts: { throttleFirstN?: number; failTransactAfter?: number } = {})`, with `let batchGetCalls = 0;` and `batchGetCalls: () => batchGetCalls` on the returned object. function draftRecord(over: Partial<AiRunRecordInput> = {}): AiRunRecordInput {
```ts
  return {
    runId: 'run-1',
    startedAt: STARTED,
    finishedAt: '2026-08-06T10:00:02.000Z',
    durationMs: 2000,
    conversationId: 'conv-1',
    contactId: 'contact-1',
    trigger: 'sms',
    outcome: 'applied',
    driver: 'fake',
    decisions: {},
    notedLines: 0,
    ...over,
  };

describe('aiRunsRepo - putRun', () => {
  it('writes ONE run# row plus one ptr# row per dimension, all sharing expires_at', async () => {
    const { doc, store } = makeFakeDoc();
    const saved = await repoWith(doc).putRun(draftRecord());

    expect(saved.itemId).toBe('run#run-1');
    const ttl = Math.floor(Date.parse(STARTED) / 1000) + 90 * 24 * 60 * 60;
    expect(saved.expires_at).toBe(ttl);

    expect([...store.keys()].sort()).toEqual([
      'ptr#contacts#contact-1#2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#run-1',
      'ptr#conversations#conv-1#2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#run-1',
      'ptr#global#2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#run-1',
      'ptr#outcome#applied#2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#run-1',
      'run#run-1',
    ]);
    for (const id of store.keys()) expect(store.get(id)!['expires_at']).toBe(ttl);
  });

  it('writes the run and its pointers ATOMICALLY - a rejected transaction leaves nothing', async () => {
    // A partial fan-out would permanently hide the run from SOME scopes while
    // showing it in others - unrepairable, in the artifact this feature exists
    // to make trustworthy.
    const { doc, store } = makeFakeDoc({ failTransactAfter: 2 });
    await expect(repoWith(doc).putRun(draftRecord())).rejects.toThrow();
    expect([...store.keys()]).toEqual([]);
  });

  it('the run# row carries NO entityKey/sortKey, so it never enters byEntity', async () => {
    const { doc, store } = makeFakeDoc();
    await repoWith(doc).putRun(draftRecord());
    expect(store.get('run#run-1')!['entityKey']).toBeUndefined();
    expect(store.get('run#run-1')!['sortKey']).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it('pointer rows are PURE pointers - keys, runId, ttl, nothing denormalized', async () => {
    const { doc, store } = makeFakeDoc();
    await repoWith(doc).putRun(draftRecord());
    const ptr = store.get('ptr#global#2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#run-1')!;
    expect(Object.keys(ptr).sort()).toEqual(['entityKey', 'expires_at', 'itemId', 'runId', 'sortKey']);
    expect(ptr['sortKey']).toBe('2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#run-1');
  });

  it('omits the contacts pointer when no contact resolved', async () => {
    const { doc, store } = makeFakeDoc();
    const input = draftRecord();
    delete (input as { contactId?: string }).contactId;
    await repoWith(doc).putRun(input);
    expect([...store.keys()].filter((k) => k.startsWith('ptr#'))).toHaveLength(3);
    expect([...store.keys()].some((k) => k.startsWith('ptr#contacts#'))).toBe(false);
  });

  it('round-trips the full record through getRun', async () => {
    const { doc } = makeFakeDoc();

    expect(got?.rawText).toBe('{"fields":{}}');

});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Write the repo (types + `putRun` + `getRun`)**

```ts
// AI run log repo - the ai_runs table (design 2026-08-06, section 5).
//
// ONE single-key table holds two kinds of item, disjoint by itemId prefix:
//   run#<runId>                          the full run record
//   ptr#<entityKey>#<startedAt>#<runId>  an adjacency pointer
//
// Only ptr# rows carry entityKey/sortKey, so the byEntity GSI is TRULY sparse
// and holds pointers ONLY. Reading a scope is Query(byEntity) newest-first then
// ONE BatchGetItem for that page's run# rows. No Scan, ever.
//
// Two BatchGetItem mechanics the reader honors, because getting either wrong is
// silent (design 5.3): responses are UNORDERED, so we re-sort by the pointer
// sortKey we already hold; and UnprocessedKeys can come back under throttling,
// so we retry with backoff before rendering a partial page.
//
// TTL: 90 days, the same expires_at on both row kinds. DynamoDB TTL is
// asynchronous and may lag up to 48h, unordered - so a pointer whose run# row
// is already reaped renders as EXPIRED rather than erroring.
//
// PII: rawText, rawResult and decision values ARE contact PII by design
// (6.2) - that is the point of the feature. They live in DynamoDB only, are
// read admin-only, and are NEVER logged. Log ids and counts only.
import {
  BatchGetCommand,
  GetCommand,
  QueryCommand,
  TransactWriteCommand,
  UpdateCommand,
  type QueryCommandInput,
} from '@aws-sdk/lib-dynamodb';
import { ConditionalCheckFailedException } from '@aws-sdk/client-dynamodb';
import { tableName } from '../lib/config.js';
import { getDocumentClient } from '../lib/dynamo.js';
import { logger as defaultLogger } from '../lib/logger.js';
import type { RepoDeps } from './conversationsRepo.js';
import type {
  DecisionTarget,
  RunDecision,
  RunWindow,
  Verdict,
} from '../services/extraction/runTypes.js';

export type RunTrigger = 'sms' | 'voice' | 'triage' | 'email';

/**
 * applied = at least one decision reached outcome `wrote` or `suggested`, OR
 *           notedLines > 0.
 * no_op   = the model was called and NOTHING was written, suggested or noted -
 *           INCLUDING the case where every finding was dropped. A run full of
 *           drops is a no_op, not an applied (design section 6).
 */
export type RunOutcome = 'applied' | 'no_op' | 'skipped' | 'failed';

/** Derived from the actual skip branches in jobs/extraction.ts (design 6.3). */
export type SkipReason = 'no_contact' | 'ineligible_type' | 'no_new_client' | 'empty_window';

/**
 * Set AT THE THROW SITE, never inferred afterwards (design 6.3).
 *   refusal  - the model declined.
 *   parse    - the response arrived but did not parse.
 *   driver   - any other failure inside extract.
 *   complete - repo.complete() threw. On ANY path, including a skip.
 *   repo     - any other repository throw on the run path (claim, getById,
 *              findByPhone, listByConversation), and the poll's backstop.
 * There is no `apply` kind: applyExtraction guards every side effect.
 */
export type RunErrorKind = 'refusal' | 'parse' | 'driver' | 'complete' | 'repo';

export interface RunError {
  kind: RunErrorKind;
  message: string;
  attempts: number;
  parked: boolean;
}

export interface RunUsage {
  inputTokens: number;
  outputTokens: number;
}

/** The stored run record. itemId and expires_at are stamped by putRun. */
export interface AiRunRecord {
  itemId: string;
  runId: string;
  /** REAL WALL-CLOCK time from the job's injected now() - NOT the poll's
   *  nowIso. The dev tick runs a SIMULATED future clock (routes/dev.ts:373
   *  advances by the debounce window), so the poll clock would stamp dev and
   *  e2e runs about one debounce into the future and mis-sort the log against
   *  real time (design section 6, as amended). */
  startedAt: string;
  finishedAt: string;
  durationMs: number;
  conversationId: string;
  contactId?: string;
  trigger: RunTrigger;
  outcome: RunOutcome;
  skipReason?: SkipReason;
  error?: RunError;
  driver: 'anthropic' | 'console' | 'fake';
  model?: string;
  /** sha256 of the assembled system prompt CONCATENATED with the serialized
   *  EXTRACTION_SCHEMA, first 12 hex - both are sent on the same call and both
   *  define the model contract (design section 6). */
  promptFingerprint?: string;
  usage?: RunUsage;
  window?: RunWindow;
  /** Populated field NAMES only - never a wholesale snapshot (6.2). */
  profileFieldsPopulated?: string[];
  rawText?: string;
  rawResult?: unknown;
  decisions: Partial<Record<DecisionTarget, RunDecision>>;
  notedLines: number;
  expires_at: number;
}

export type AiRunRecordInput = Omit<AiRunRecord, 'itemId' | 'expires_at'>;

/** An adjacency pointer. PURE pointer - no denormalized run summary, because a
 *  copied summary would need updating whenever a verdict lands, and a drifting
 *  second copy is the bug class this feature exists to catch. */
export interface AiRunPointer {
  itemId: string;
  entityKey: string;
  sortKey: string;
  runId: string;
  expires_at: number;
}

/** One list row: the run, or a tombstone for a pointer whose run row TTLd. */
export type AiRunListEntry =
  | { runId: string; sortKey: string; expired: false; run: AiRunRecord }
  | { runId: string; sortKey: string; expired: true };

export interface ListByEntityOptions {
  /** Page size. Defaults to DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE (25). */
  limit?: number;
  /** Exclusive upper bound on sortKey - the previous page's last sortKey. */
  before?: string;
  /** Inclusive lower bound on sortKey (a date-range floor, `<ISO>`). */
  from?: string;
  /** Inclusive upper bound on sortKey (a date-range ceiling, `<ISO>`). */
  to?: string;
}

export interface AiRunsRepo {
  /** Write the run# row and every ptr# row in ONE transaction. NOT idempotent
   *  by design: a retried run really did call the model twice (design 8). */
  putRun(input: AiRunRecordInput): Promise<AiRunRecord>;
  getRun(runId: string): Promise<AiRunRecord | undefined>;
  /** One Query on byEntity (newest-first) + ONE BatchGetItem, re-sorted by the
   *  pointer sortKey and retried on UnprocessedKeys. */
  listByEntity(
    entityKey: string,
    opts?: ListByEntityOptions,
  ): Promise<{ entries: AiRunListEntry[]; nextBefore?: string }>;
  /** Stamp a verdict on ONE decision. False when the run row is gone (TTL'd). */
  setVerdict(
    runId: string,
    target: DecisionTarget,
    verdict: Verdict,
    opts?: { at?: string; by?: string },
  ): Promise<boolean>;
}

/** Retention (design section 10). */
export const RUN_TTL_DAYS = 90;
/** The page size the reader and the API default to (design 5.3, 9). */
export const DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE = 25;
/** Above every printable ASCII character, so `<to>` + this bounds a range. */
const SORT_KEY_CEILING = '\uffff';
/** BatchGetItem UnprocessedKeys retry budget + first backoff step. */
const MAX_BATCH_ATTEMPTS = 4;
const BATCH_BACKOFF_MS = 25;

const sleep = (ms: number): Promise<void> => new Promise((resolve) => setTimeout(resolve, ms));

export const runItemId = (runId: string): string => `run#${runId}`;
export const runSortKey = (startedAt: string, runId: string): string => `${startedAt}#${runId}`;
export const pointerItemId = (entityKey: string, startedAt: string, runId: string): string =>
  `ptr#${entityKey}#${runSortKey(startedAt, runId)}`;

/**
 * Every dimension this run should be filterable by (design 5.3). `global` and
 * `outcome#<outcome>` are LITERAL SCOPE TOKENS; the rest use the `<table>#<id>`
 * convention audit_events uses. Both forms coexist in this index by design.
 */
export function pointerEntityKeys(
  input: Pick<AiRunRecordInput, 'outcome' | 'conversationId' | 'contactId'>,
): string[] {
  return [
    'global',
    `outcome#${input.outcome}`,
    `conversations#${input.conversationId}`,
    ...(input.contactId !== undefined ? [`contacts#${input.contactId}`] : []),
  ];
}

/** Epoch seconds, RUN_TTL_DAYS after startedAt. Same value on both row kinds. */
export function runExpiresAt(startedAt: string): number {
  return Math.floor(Date.parse(startedAt) / 1000) + RUN_TTL_DAYS * 24 * 60 * 60;
}

export function createAiRunsRepo(deps: RepoDeps = {}): AiRunsRepo {
  const doc = deps.doc ?? getDocumentClient();
  const table = tableName('ai_runs', deps.env);
  const log = deps.logger ?? defaultLogger;

  /**
   * One BatchGetItem for a page of run# rows, retrying UnprocessedKeys with
   * backoff before we render a partial page (design 5.3). A key still
   * unprocessed after MAX_BATCH_ATTEMPTS is simply absent from the map and the
   * caller renders it as expired - a degraded row beats a failed page.
   */
  async function batchGetRuns(runIds: string[]): Promise<Map<string, AiRunRecord>> {
    const found = new Map<string, AiRunRecord>();
    let keys = runIds.map((runId) => ({ itemId: runItemId(runId) }));
    for (let attempt = 0; attempt < MAX_BATCH_ATTEMPTS && keys.length > 0; attempt += 1) {
      if (attempt > 0) await sleep(BATCH_BACKOFF_MS * 2 ** (attempt - 1));
      const res = await doc.send(new BatchGetCommand({ RequestItems: { [table]: { Keys: keys } } }));
      for (const item of (res.Responses?.[table] ?? []) as AiRunRecord[]) found.set(item.runId, item);
      keys = (res.UnprocessedKeys?.[table]?.Keys ?? []) as Array<{ itemId: string }>;
    }
    if (keys.length > 0) {
      log.warn({ unprocessed: keys.length }, 'ai run log: BatchGet left keys unprocessed after retries');
    }
    return found;
  }

  return {
    async putRun(input) {
      const expires_at = runExpiresAt(input.startedAt);
      const record: AiRunRecord = { ...input, itemId: runItemId(input.runId), expires_at };
      const pointers: AiRunPointer[] = pointerEntityKeys(input).map((entityKey) => ({
        itemId: pointerItemId(entityKey, input.startedAt, input.runId),
        entityKey,
        sortKey: runSortKey(input.startedAt, input.runId),
        runId: input.runId,
        expires_at,
      }));
      // ATOMIC: the run and every pointer land together or not at all. A partial
      // fan-out would permanently hide the run from some scopes while showing it
      // in others. Five items is far under the 100-item transaction limit.
      await doc.send(
        new TransactWriteCommand({
          TransactItems: [
            { Put: { TableName: table, Item: record } },
            ...pointers.map((Item) => ({ Put: { TableName: table, Item } })),
          ],
        }),
      );
      // Ids + counts only. NEVER the record (it carries PII by design).
      log.debug(
        { runId: input.runId, conversationId: input.conversationId, outcome: input.outcome },
        'ai run recorded',
      );
      return record;
    },

    async getRun(runId) {
      const { Item } = await doc.send(
        new GetCommand({ TableName: table, Key: { itemId: runItemId(runId) } }),
      );
      return Item as AiRunRecord | undefined;
    },

    async listByEntity() {
      throw new Error('not implemented'); // Task 4
    },

    async setVerdict() {
      throw new Error('not implemented'); // Task 5
    },
  };
}
```

Leaving the two unimplemented methods as explicit throws (rather than silent stubs) means an accidental early caller fails loudly.

```ts
describe('aiRunsRepo - listByEntity', () => {
  async function seed(repo: AiRunsRepo, n: number): Promise<void> {
    for (let i = 0; i < n; i += 1) {
      await repo.putRun(
        draftRecord({
          runId: `run-${String(i).padStart(2, '0')}`,

    expect(entries.map((e) => e.runId)).toEqual(['run-04', 'run-03', 'run-02', 'run-01', 'run-00']);

    const { doc } = makeFakeDoc();
    const repo = repoWith(doc);
    await seed(repo, 30);
    const page1 = await repo.listByEntity('global');
    expect(page1.entries).toHaveLength(25);
    expect(page1.nextBefore).toBe(page1.entries[24]!.sortKey);
    const page2 = await repo.listByEntity('global', { before: page1.nextBefore });
    expect(page2.entries).toHaveLength(5);
    expect(page2.nextBefore).toBeUndefined();
    expect(new Set([...page1.entries, ...page2.entries].map((e) => e.runId)).size).toBe(30);
  });

  it('retries UnprocessedKeys before rendering a partial page', async () => {
    const { doc, batchGetCalls } = makeFakeDoc({ throttleFirstN: 2 });
    const repo = repoWith(doc);
    await seed(repo, 6);
    const { entries } = await repo.listByEntity('global');
    expect(entries).toHaveLength(6);
    expect(entries.every((e) => e.expired === false)).toBe(true);
    expect(batchGetCalls()).toBeGreaterThan(1);
  });

    const { doc, store } = makeFakeDoc();
    const repo = repoWith(doc);
    await seed(repo, 3);

    expect(entries.map((e) => `${e.runId}:${String(e.expired)}`)).toEqual([

  });

    const { doc } = makeFakeDoc();

    expect(await ids('outcome#applied')).toEqual(['a']);
    expect(await ids('outcome#no_op')).toEqual(['b']);
    expect(await ids('conversations#conv-2')).toEqual(['b']);
    expect(await ids('contacts#contact-1')).toEqual(['a']);
    expect(await ids('global')).toEqual(['b', 'a']);

  it('narrows to a date range on sortKey', async () => {
    const { doc } = makeFakeDoc();
    const repo = repoWith(doc);
    await seed(repo, 5); // 10:00 .. 10:04
    const { entries } = await repo.listByEntity('global', {
      from: '2026-08-06T10:01:00.000Z',
      to: '2026-08-06T10:03:00.000Z',
    });
    expect(entries.map((e) => e.runId)).toEqual(['run-03', 'run-02', 'run-01']);

  it('returns an empty page for an unknown scope without erroring', async () => {

    const out = await repoWith(doc).listByEntity('contacts#nobody');
    expect(out.entries).toEqual([]);
    expect(out.nextBefore).toBeUndefined();
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
    async listByEntity(entityKey, opts = {}) {
      const limit = opts.limit ?? DEFAULT_PAGE_SIZE;
      // Range bounds on sortKey (`<startedAt ISO>#<runId>`). `to` is inclusive
      // of the whole second, so it is ceilinged with a char above every
      // printable ASCII one; `before` is the previous page's last sortKey and is
      // EXCLUSIVE. DynamoDB's BETWEEN is spelled as two comparisons here so the
      // key condition stays a plain AND chain.
      const upper =
        opts.before !== undefined
          ? { expr: '#sk < :upper', value: opts.before }
          : opts.to !== undefined
            ? { expr: '#sk <= :upper', value: `${opts.to}${SORT_KEY_CEILING}` }
            : undefined;

      const input: QueryCommandInput = {
        TableName: table,
        IndexName: 'byEntity',
        KeyConditionExpression: [
          '#ek = :ek',
          ...(upper !== undefined ? [upper.expr] : []),
          ...(opts.from !== undefined ? ['#sk >= :lower'] : []),
        ].join(' AND '),
        ExpressionAttributeNames: { '#ek': 'entityKey', '#sk': 'sortKey' },
        ExpressionAttributeValues: {
          ':ek': entityKey,
          ...(upper !== undefined && { ':upper': upper.value }),
          ...(opts.from !== undefined && { ':lower': opts.from }),
        },
        ScanIndexForward: false, // newest-first
        Limit: limit,
      };
      const page = await doc.send(new QueryCommand(input));
      const pointers = ((page.Items as AiRunPointer[] | undefined) ?? []).map((p) => ({
        runId: p.runId,
        sortKey: p.sortKey,
      }));
      if (pointers.length === 0) return { entries: [] };

      const byRunId = await batchGetRuns(pointers.map((p) => p.runId));

      // Re-sort by the pointer order we already hold. BatchGetItem responses are
      // UNORDERED - never trust them (design 5.3).
      const entries: AiRunListEntry[] = pointers.map((p) => {
        const run = byRunId.get(p.runId);
        return run !== undefined
          ? { runId: p.runId, sortKey: p.sortKey, expired: false, run }
          : { runId: p.runId, sortKey: p.sortKey, expired: true };
      });

      const nextBefore =
        entries.length === limit && page.LastEvaluatedKey !== undefined
          ? entries[entries.length - 1]!.sortKey
          : undefined;
      return { entries, ...(nextBefore !== undefined && { nextBefore }) };
    },
```

### Task 5: `setVerdict` with the `attribute_exists` guard

**Files:** Modify `app/src/repos/aiRunsRepo.ts` (replace the `setVerdict` throw), `app/test/aiRunsRepo.test.ts` (append)

**Interfaces:** Produces `setVerdict(runId, target, verdict, { at?, by? }) -> Promise<boolean>` (`true` stamped, `false` run gone). Consumed by Tasks 21-23.

**Precondition, stated in the JSDoc:** a nested-path `SET decisions.<target>.verdict` requires the `decisions.<target>` MAP to already exist. It always does - `setVerdict` is only ever called with a `(runId, target)` pair read off a suggestion row, and that suggestion exists only because the run recorded a decision with outcome `suggested` for that target.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
    /**
     * PRECONDITION: `decisions.<target>` must already exist on the run row. It
     * always does - setVerdict is only ever called with a (runId, target) read
     * off a suggestion row, and that suggestion exists only because the run
     * recorded a decision with outcome 'suggested' for that target.
     */
    async setVerdict(runId, target, verdict, opts = {}) {
      const at = opts.at ?? new Date().toISOString();
      const sets = ['#d.#t.#v = :v', '#d.#t.#va = :at'];
      const values: Record<string, unknown> = { ':v': verdict, ':at': at };
      const names: Record<string, string> = {
        '#d': 'decisions', '#t': target, '#v': 'verdict', '#va': 'verdictAt',
      };
      if (opts.by !== undefined) {
        sets.push('#d.#t.#vb = :by');
        values[':by'] = opts.by;
        names['#vb'] = 'verdictBy';
      }
      try {
        await doc.send(
          new UpdateCommand({
            TableName: table,
            Key: { itemId: runItemId(runId) },
            UpdateExpression: `SET ${sets.join(', ')}`,
            // Runs TTL at 90 days but pending suggestions never expire, so a
            // verdict can land on a reaped run. Without this guard a
            // nested-path UpdateItem either errors or UPSERTS a malformed
            // partial item (design 7.3).
            ConditionExpression: 'attribute_exists(itemId)',
            ExpressionAttributeNames: names,
            ExpressionAttributeValues: values,
          }),
        );
        log.debug({ runId, target, verdict }, 'ai run verdict stamped');
        return true;
      } catch (err) {
        if (err instanceof ConditionalCheckFailedException) {
          log.debug({ runId, target }, 'ai run verdict skipped (run row expired)');
          return false;
        }
        throw err;
      }
    },
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Refactor `prompt.ts`**

Change the type-only import at `:7` to `import type { ExtractionInput, TranscriptUtterance } from '../../adapters/extraction.js';`, then replace `:93-98`:

```ts
/**
 * Render ONE utterance to its transcript line. THE single renderer: the
 * extraction request is built from it (below) and the run log hashes its output
 * (services/extraction/runWindow.ts), so the recorded hash and the sent bytes
 * can never diverge. Reconstructing this format anywhere else would guarantee a
 * permanent false hash mismatch (design 2026-08-06 section 6.1).
 *
 * Deliberately does NOT render tsMsgId - the wire format is fixed by the system
 * prompt at line 20 ("<timestamp> [<speaker>/<channel>] <text>").
 */
export function renderUtteranceLine(u: TranscriptUtterance): string {
  return `${u.at} [${u.speaker}/${u.channel}] ${toSingleLine(u.text)}`;
}

export function buildExtractionUserContent(input: ExtractionInput): string {
  const profileJson = JSON.stringify(input.profile, null, 2);
  // GLOBAL sort by timestamp. All utterances of ONE message share that
  // message's timestamp (toUtterances stamps a call's lines with the call row's
  // created_at), and Array.prototype.sort is stable, so per-message order is
  // preserved - which is what makes the per-message hash in runWindow.ts match.
  const ordered = [...input.transcript].sort((a, b) => (a.at < b.at ? -1 : a.at > b.at ? 1 : 0));
  const lines = ordered.map(renderUtteranceLine);
  return ['CURRENT PROFILE', profileJson, '', 'TRANSCRIPT', ...lines].join('\n');
}
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the field**

Replace `app/src/adapters/extraction.ts:21-32`:

```ts
export interface TranscriptUtterance {
  /**
   * The tsMsgId of the stored message this utterance came from. REQUIRED, not
   * optional: an optional id would let a construction site omit it and silently
   * produce a message the run log cannot hash (design 2026-08-06 section 6.1).
   * A call transcript yields many utterances - all share the call row's id.
   * NEVER rendered into the prompt (services/extraction/prompt.ts).
   */
  tsMsgId: string;
  // 'unknown' is a call line labeled `Speaker N:` (legacy/underivable role) -
  // the extraction job assigns it (adapters/extractionFake skips non-client
  // speakers; the prompt renders the label verbatim).
  speaker: 'staff' | 'client' | 'unknown';
  text: string;
  at: string; // ISO 8601
  // NOTE (ADJ-13): this is the TRANSCRIPT channel - a different union from the
  // extraction-repo SCHEDULING channel ('sms'|'voice'|'triage'|'email').
  // 'triage' is a scheduling trigger, never transcript content - do not add it.
  channel: 'sms' | 'voice' | 'email';
}
```

# SLICE 4 - BREAKING 2: the discriminated `ExtractionCall`

Spec 6.4: `extract` widens to return the result plus a metadata envelope, as a DISCRIMINATED value rather than a throw - so the caller knows which stage failed, and a thrown error carrying `rawText` never lands on the object loggers serialize wholesale.

**This slice deliberately does NOT change job behavior.** The single call site gets a TEMPORARY unwrap that re-throws on `ok:false`, preserving today's backoff/park/attempts semantics exactly. Task 19 removes it; **Task 11's regression test** pins the numbers across both.

**Fallout inventory (verified):** 3 real drivers (`app/src/adapters/extraction.ts:113`/`:121-127` console, `:130`/`:143-171` anthropic; `app/src/adapters/extractionFake.ts:16`/`:24-41`); 1 production call site (`app/src/jobs/extraction.ts:364`); **4 test doubles at `app/test/extractionJob.test.ts:179-187`, `:334-339`, `:358-363`, `:379-384`, ALL `as`-casts that TypeScript will NOT flag**; 5 return-shape assertions at `app/test/extractionAdapter.test.ts:34`, `:64`, `:80`, `:91`, `:102`.

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
/**
 * sha256 of the assembled system prompt CONCATENATED with the serialized
 * EXTRACTION_SCHEMA, first 12 hex (design 2026-08-06 section 6). Both are sent
 * on the same messages.create call and both define the model contract, so
 * fingerprinting the prompt alone would miss half of it.
 *
 * Memoized: both inputs are module constants, and this is read on every run and
 * on every System Status flags request.
 */
let promptFingerprintCache: string | undefined;
export function extractionPromptFingerprint(): string {
  promptFingerprintCache ??= createHash('sha256')
    .update(buildExtractionSystemPrompt(), 'utf8')
    .update(JSON.stringify(EXTRACTION_SCHEMA), 'utf8')
    .digest('hex')
    .slice(0, 12);
  return promptFingerprintCache;
}
```

### Task 9: Widen `extract` to return `ExtractionCall`

**Files:** Modify `app/src/adapters/extraction.ts:14`, `:100-103`, `:106`, `:121-127`, `:143-171`; `app/src/adapters/extractionFake.ts:12`, `:24-41`; `app/test/extractionAdapter.test.ts:34`, `:64`, `:80`, `:91`, `:102`

**Interfaces:** Produces

```ts
export interface ExtractionMeta {
  driver: 'anthropic' | 'console' | 'fake';
  model?: string;
  rawText?: string;
  usage?: { inputTokens: number; outputTokens: number };
  promptFingerprint?: string;
}
export type ExtractionCall =
  | { ok: true; meta: ExtractionMeta; result: ExtractionResult }
  | { ok: false; meta: ExtractionMeta; failure: 'refusal' | 'parse' | 'driver'; message: string };
export interface ExtractionDriver {
  readonly kind: 'anthropic' | 'console' | 'fake';
  extract(input: ExtractionInput): Promise<ExtractionCall>;
}
```

Consumed by Tasks 10, 11, 19.

**`meta.rawText` on the fake driver is a HARD CONTRACT.** It is the marker PAYLOAD only - `markerLine.slice('EXTRACT:'.length)` - so it is REAL JSON, never the `EXTRACT:`-prefixed line. If the prefix leaked in, `parseExtractionOps` would fail to parse it, return the empty view, and record EVERY e2e decision as `not_addressed`: the e2e would pass while verifying nothing. Two tests below pin it, and Task 30's e2e asserts a real `no_finding` to prove the mechanism ran.

**Marker payloads must be SCHEMA-SHAPED from here on.** Because `rawText` is now read by `parseExtractionOps`, every `EXTRACT:` payload written by this plan (tests and e2e) uses the wire shape - `{"fields":{"<name>":{"op":"...","value":"...","reason":"..."}}}`, `{"typeSuggestion":{"value":"...","reason":""}}`, `{"statusAdvance":{"suggest":true,"reason":""}}`, `{"phoneAddition":{"phone":"...","label":"","reason":""}}`, `{"address":{"op":"...","line1":"...","reason":""}}` - not an ad-hoc partial. `op` sits at the same path in the internal and wire shapes, so this stays compatible with the fake driver's `Partial<ExtractionResult>` merge.

- [ ] **Step 3: Widen the interface and the console driver**

Replace `app/src/adapters/extraction.ts:100-103`:

```ts
/** Everything about the CALL, independent of whether it succeeded. */
export interface ExtractionMeta {
  driver: 'anthropic' | 'console' | 'fake';
  /** The model id actually called. Absent on console/fake. */
  model?: string;
  /** The model's response text, VERBATIM, pre-parse. REAL JSON - on the fake
   *  driver this is the EXTRACT: marker PAYLOAD, never the prefixed line, so
   *  parseExtractionOps can read it. Absent on console, and on a driver failure
   *  before a response arrived. PII by design. */
  rawText?: string;
  usage?: { inputTokens: number; outputTokens: number };
  promptFingerprint?: string;
}

/**
 * A DISCRIMINATED result rather than a throw, for two reasons (design 6.4): the
 * caller must know WHICH STAGE failed, and a thrown error carrying rawText
 * would put contact PII on the one object type this codebase's loggers
 * serialize wholesale (`logger.error({ err })`).
 *
 * It also saves the case a throwing parse loses outright: meta is assembled
 * FIRST, so rawText survives a parse failure - the single failure mode where
 * the response text is the entire answer to "what went wrong".
 */
export type ExtractionCall =
  | { ok: true; meta: ExtractionMeta; result: ExtractionResult }
  | { ok: false; meta: ExtractionMeta; failure: 'refusal' | 'parse' | 'driver'; message: string };

export interface ExtractionDriver {
  readonly kind: 'anthropic' | 'console' | 'fake';
  extract(input: ExtractionInput): Promise<ExtractionCall>;
}
```

Replace the console driver's `extract` (`:121-127`):

```ts
  async extract(input: ExtractionInput): Promise<ExtractionCall> {
    this.log.info(
      { transcriptLength: input.transcript.length, contactType: input.profile.contactType },
      'console extraction driver: returning empty result (offline)',
    );
    // No model, no response text, no usage - it never called anything. The run
    // log records `driver: console` and leaves the rest absent, and with no
    // rawText NO target may be recorded as no_finding (design 7.1).
    return { ok: true, meta: { driver: 'console' }, result: EMPTY_EXTRACTION };
  }
```

Update the `ExtractionRefusedError` doc at `:106`: `/** The model declined to answer. The anthropic driver now RETURNS { ok:false, failure:'refusal' } instead of throwing this; the class survives as the error the job's temporary unwrap raises and as a stable instanceof for callers. */`

- [ ] **Step 4: Rewrite the anthropic driver**

Add `extractionPromptFingerprint` to the `../services/extraction/prompt.js` import at `:14`, then replace `:143-171`:

```ts
  async extract(input: ExtractionInput): Promise<ExtractionCall> {
    // Meta is assembled INCREMENTALLY and returned on every path, so a failure
    // never discards what we already learned (design 6.4).
    const meta: ExtractionMeta = {
      driver: 'anthropic',
      model: this.model,
      promptFingerprint: extractionPromptFingerprint(),
    };
    let message: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof this.client.messages.create>>;
    try {
      message = await this.client.messages.create({
        model: this.model,
        max_tokens: MAX_OUTPUT_TOKENS,
        output_config: { format: { type: 'json_schema', schema: EXTRACTION_SCHEMA } },
        system: buildExtractionSystemPrompt(),
        messages: [{ role: 'user', content: buildExtractionUserContent(input) }],
      });
    } catch (err) {
      return { ok: false, meta, failure: 'driver', message: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) };
    }
    meta.usage = { inputTokens: message.usage.input_tokens, outputTokens: message.usage.output_tokens };
    // Per-run token spend (cost observability for the input caps). Counts only -
    // never transcript text (PII).
    this.log.info(
      {
        inputTokens: message.usage.input_tokens,
        outputTokens: message.usage.output_tokens,
        transcriptUtterances: input.transcript.length,
      },
      'anthropic extraction usage',
    );
    if (message.stop_reason === 'refusal') {
      return {
        ok: false, meta, failure: 'refusal',
        message: 'Anthropic declined to extract (stop_reason: refusal)',
      };
    }
    const textBlock = message.content.find(
      (block): block is Extract<typeof block, { type: 'text' }> => block.type === 'text',
    );
    if (!textBlock) {
      return {
        ok: false, meta, failure: 'driver',
        message: 'Anthropic extraction response contained no text block',
      };
    }
    // rawText is stamped BEFORE the parse, so a malformed response still carries
    // the text that explains it.
    meta.rawText = textBlock.text;
    try {
      return { ok: true, meta, result: parseExtractionText(textBlock.text) };
    } catch (err) {
      return { ok: false, meta, failure: 'parse', message: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) };
    }
  }
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Rewrite the fake driver**

Add `ExtractionCall` and `ExtractionMeta` to the type import at `app/src/adapters/extractionFake.ts:12`, then replace `:24-41`:

```ts
  async extract(input: ExtractionInput): Promise<ExtractionCall> {
    // Newest-first by timestamp (robust to caller ordering).
    const newestFirst = [...input.transcript].sort((a, b) => (a.at < b.at ? 1 : a.at > b.at ? -1 : 0));
    for (const utterance of newestFirst) {
      if (utterance.speaker !== 'client') continue;
      const markerLine = utterance.text.split(/\r?\n/).find((line) => line.startsWith(MARKER));
      if (markerLine === undefined) continue;
      // The marker PAYLOAD only - real JSON, never the EXTRACT:-prefixed line.
      // parseExtractionOps reads this, and a leaked prefix would make it return
      // the empty view and record every decision as not_addressed (design 7.1).
      const json = markerLine.slice(MARKER.length);
      const meta: ExtractionMeta = { driver: 'fake', rawText: json };
      try {
        const partial = JSON.parse(json) as Partial<ExtractionResult>;
        return { ok: true, meta, result: { fields: {}, ...partial } };
      } catch (err) {
        // Behavior UNCHANGED: a malformed marker is an EMPTY result, never a
        // failure and never a throw. rawText still rides along.
        this.log.warn({ err }, 'fake extraction driver: malformed EXTRACT marker JSON, returning empty result');
        return { ok: true, meta, result: { fields: {} } };
      }
    }
    return { ok: true, meta: { driver: 'fake' }, result: { fields: {} } };
  }
```

# SLICE 5 - BREAKING 3: `putSuggestion` returns the displaced row; suggestions carry `runId`

Spec 7.3: the `superseded` stamp is NOT written inside `putSuggestion` and NOT by `apply.ts` (apply is `putSuggestion`'s caller, so "the caller does it" would hand apply an `ai_runs` dependency the write-path section denies). Instead `putSuggestion` RETURNS the displaced row, `applyExtraction` surfaces the displaced `runId`s, and the JOB writes the stamps (Slice 11).

```ts
        for (const [k, v] of Object.entries(item)) if (v !== undefined) stored[k] = v;
        store.set(item['itemId'] as string, stored);
        return cmd.input.ReturnValues === 'ALL_OLD' && previous !== undefined
          ? { Attributes: { ...previous } }
          : {};
      }
```

```ts
/** What putSuggestion hands back: the row it wrote, plus the row it REPLACED. */
export interface PutSuggestionResult {
  item: SuggestionItem;
  /**
   * The pending suggestion this upsert displaced, when there was one. SURFACED,
   * never acted on: the JOB stamps the displaced run's decision `superseded`, so
   * apply.ts keeps NO ai_runs dependency and a stamp failure can never be
   * mistaken for a suggestion failure (design 7.3).

  displaced?: SuggestionItem;
}
```

`ApplyOutcome` (`:43-50`) - ADD only, so no caller breaks:

```ts
  /**
   * Earlier runs whose pending suggestion THIS run displaced. Surfaced, never
   * acted on: the JOB stamps those runs' decisions `superseded`, so apply keeps
   * NO ai_runs dependency and a stamp failure can never be mistaken for a
   * suggestion failure - which matters, because putSuggestionSafe treats a
   * throw as a failed suggestion and that suppresses the load-bearing
   * `suggestion.updated` emit below (design 7.3).
   */
  displaced: Array<{ target: string; runId: string }>;
```

`ctx` (`:127-139`):

```ts
    /** The ai_runs runId this apply belongs to. Stamped onto every suggestion so
     *  every verdict surface knows which run record to address. Optional so
     *  non-job callers and older tests are unaffected. */
    runId?: string;
```

`putSuggestionSafe` (`:450-477`) - widen the result so a failure carries WHY, instead of the boolean that threw the information away:

```ts
/**
 * Best-effort putSuggestion. Returns the displaced row on success, or a DROP
 * REASON on failure - so the run log can record why a suggestion never appeared.
 */
type SafePutResult =
  | { ok: true; displaced?: SuggestionItem }
  | { ok: false; dropReason: 'dismissed_before' | 'repo_error' };

async function putSuggestionSafe(
  deps: ApplyDeps,
  s: Parameters<ApplyDeps['extraction']['putSuggestion']>[0],
): Promise<SafePutResult> {
  try {
    // Dismissal tombstone check (single choke point for EVERY suggest path): a
    // value a human already rejected for this target is never re-suggested
    // (permanent by ruling 2026-07-21); a different value still comes through.
    const norm = normalizeSuggestionValue(s.target, s.suggestedValue);
    if (await deps.extraction.hasDismissal(s.ownerContactId, s.target, norm)) {
      deps.logger.debug(
        { contactId: s.ownerContactId, target: s.target },
        'suggestion suppressed (previously dismissed value)',
      );
      return { ok: false, dropReason: 'dismissed_before' };
    }
    const { displaced } = await deps.extraction.putSuggestion(s);
    return { ok: true, ...(displaced !== undefined && { displaced }) };
  } catch (err) {
    deps.logger.warn({ contactId: s.ownerContactId, target: s.target, err }, 'putSuggestion failed');
    return { ok: false, dropReason: 'repo_error' };
  }
}
```

Import `type SuggestionItem` from `../../repos/extractionRepo.js` (type-only, no runtime edge).

Next to `const wrote: string[] = [];` (`:145`):

```ts
  const displaced: ApplyOutcome['displaced'] = [];
  /** Record a displaced pending suggestion, when it carried a runId. Rows
   *  written before the run log existed have none and are simply skipped. */
  const noteDisplaced = (target: string, prior: SuggestionItem | undefined): void => {
    if (prior?.runId !== undefined) displaced.push({ target, runId: prior.runId });
  };
```

# SLICE 6 - `ApplyOutcome` per-target decisions and drop reasons

Spec 8: "`applyExtraction` widens its `ApplyOutcome` to return per-target outcomes, drop reasons, and any displaced `runId`s; the job joins those with the model's response to build `decisions`." Every branch below ends in `logger.debug` today and is gone - which is why spec 11 notes these branches have no test coverage at all. **Purely ADDITIVE**: `wrote`, `suggested`, `notedLines`, `displaced` all stay.

Note that `empty_value_at_parse` is NOT in this table: apply never sees those ops, because `schema.ts` folded them away first. Task 17 assigns it.

### Task 14: Record an `ApplyDecision` at every write, suggest and discard branch

**Files:** Modify `app/src/services/extraction/apply.ts:43-50` and the branches below; `app/test/extractionApply.test.ts` (append)

**Interfaces:** Consumes `DropReason` from `runTypes.ts` (Task 2). **`apply.ts` imports the vocabulary module ONLY - never `aiRunsRepo.ts`.** Produces:

```ts
export interface ApplyDecision {
  target: string;
  outcome: 'wrote' | 'suggested' | 'dropped';
  proposedValue?: string;
  coercedValue?: unknown;
  previousValue?: string;
  reason?: string;
  demotedFrom?: 'write';
  dropReason?: DropReason;
}
// ApplyOutcome gains:  decisions: ApplyDecision[];
```

Consumed by Tasks 17, 19.

**Branch table - every mutation and discard surface, each an explicit edit:**

| `apply.ts` | Branch | Emit |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `:168-171` | field op, `fieldApplies` false | `dropped` / `wrong_contact_type` |
| `:173-177` | field op, `coerceField` invalid | `dropped` / `invalid_value` (do NOT copy `coerced.reason` into `reason` - `reason` is the MODEL's reason) |
| `:179-188` | field op, direct write | `wrote` + `previousValue`/`coercedValue`/`proposedValue`/`reason` - via `pendingDecisions`, see the last row |
| `:193` | write demoted by inferred-role | `demotedFrom: 'write'` on the decision that branch goes on to emit |
| `:195-200` | field suggest equal to current | `dropped` / `equal_to_current` |
| `:201-211` | field suggest upserted | `suggested`, or `dropped` + `put.dropReason` |
| `:220-222` | address, contact not tenant | `dropped` / `wrong_contact_type` |
| `:225-227` | address, no usable parts | **DO NOT EMIT.** Unreachable - `schema.ts:258-263` drops a zero-part address before apply runs. Recording a value that can never occur is the phantom class this design removed (spec 7.2). Leave the `logger.debug`. |
| `:244-255` | address direct write | `wrote`, `previousValue: formattedCurrent` when `hasCurrent`, `coercedValue: parts` - via `pendingDecisions` |
| `:261-262` | address write demoted by inferred-role | `demotedFrom: 'write'` |
| `:263-265` | address write demoted because LOSSY | `demotedFrom: 'write'` (spec 7.2: `lossy_address` is NOT a drop reason - it produces a suggestion) |
| `:266-271` | address suggest equal to current | `dropped` / `equal_to_current` |
| `:272-283` | address suggest upserted | `suggested`, or `dropped` + `put.dropReason` |
| `:327-337` | statusAdvance upserted | `suggested`, `previousValue: contact.status`, `coercedValue: 'searching'` |
| `:338-343` | statusAdvance, not an onboarding tenant | `dropped` / `status_not_onboarding_tenant` |
| `:348-358` | typeSuggestion upserted | `suggested`, `previousValue: contact.type` |
| `:359-361` | typeSuggestion, already classified | `dropped` / `type_already_classified` |
| `:367-368` | phone not canonicalizable | `dropped` / `phone_not_canonicalizable` |
| `:369-370` | phone already owned by this contact | `dropped` / `phone_already_owned` |
| `:379-381` | phone owned by ANOTHER contact | `dropped` / `phone_owned_by_other`, **and keep the note line** - two different mutations, both recorded (spec 7.2) |
| `:389-397` | phone upserted | `suggested`, or `dropped` + `put.dropReason` |
| `:409-411` | note-line address filter | **DO NOT EMIT.** Note lines are not targets - a key here would be an orphan. Filtered notes are visible only as a lower `notedLines` (spec 7.2). |
| `:291-307` | the batched `contacts.update` FAILING | **Load-bearing.** `wrote` is only pushed after the update lands (`:294`). Build write decisions into a `pendingDecisions` array alongside `pendingWrites` and append them to `decisions` ONLY inside the `try`, right after `wrote.push(...pendingWrites)`. A write that never landed must not read as `wrote`. |

```ts
    expect(out.notedLines).toBe(1);

  it('EXHAUSTIVENESS: every branch that mutates or discards emits exactly one decision', async () => {
    // Guards the Task 17 join: a target apply forgets to report is recorded as a
    // REASONLESS drop and warns, rather than silently reading as something else.
    // If this test ever fails, apply gained a branch and the table in the plan's

      fields: {
        pets: { op: 'suggest', value: 'two cats' },      // suggested
        voucherSize: { op: 'write', value: '99' },        // dropped invalid_value
        tenure: { op: 'none' },                           // no branch at all

    expect(out.decisions.map((d) => d.target).sort()).toEqual(['pets', 'voucherSize']);
    for (const d of out.decisions) {
      if (d.outcome === 'dropped') expect(d.dropReason).toBeDefined();
    }
  });
});
```

```ts
export interface ApplyDecision {
  target: string;
  outcome: 'wrote' | 'suggested' | 'dropped';
  /** The raw string the model supplied. */

}
```

# SLICE 7 - `parseExtractionOps`, TOTAL

Spec 7.1: ALL TWELVE targets are read from `rawText`, never from the parsed result, because `parseExtractionText` folds the address and type sentinels to ABSENT (`schema.ts:233`, `:258-263`) and REWRITES a value-less write/suggest into `{ op: 'none' }` while dropping the reason (`schema.ts:215-219`). `schema.ts` owns the raw view so the sentinel contract is never re-encoded in a second place.

### Task 15: `parseExtractionOps(rawText)` in `schema.ts`

**Files:** Modify `app/src/services/extraction/schema.ts` (append; add `import { DECISION_TARGETS, type DecisionTarget, type ProposedOp } from './runTypes.js';`); create `app/test/extractionOps.test.ts`; unskip the Task 9 test.

**Interfaces:** Produces `ExtractionOpView`, `ExtractionOpsView`, `EMPTY_OPS_VIEW`, `parseExtractionOps(rawText: string | undefined): ExtractionOpsView`. Consumed by Tasks 17, 19.

**Per-target mapping. Read the RAW JSON; no folding, no downgrading.**

| Target | Source | Rule |
| --- | --- | --- |
| the eight scalars | `root.fields[<name>]` | object whose `op` is `none`/`write`/`suggest` -> that op. Anything else -> `absent`. |
| `address` | `root.address` | object whose `op` is `none`/`write`/`suggest` -> that op (NO parts folding). Anything else -> `absent`. |
| `status` | `root.statusAdvance` | object with boolean `suggest` -> `true` gives `suggest`, `false` gives `none`. Anything else -> `absent`. |
| `type` | `root.typeSuggestion` | object with a non-empty string `value` -> `'none'` gives `none`; ANY other string gives `suggest` carrying that raw value (an off-enum value is the model TRYING and failing, and must never read as a decline). Anything else -> `absent`. |
| `phone` | `root.phoneAddition` | object with a string `phone` -> non-empty-after-trim gives `suggest` with `value: phone`; empty gives `none`. Anything else -> `absent`. |

**`value` and `reason` retention, one rule for all targets:** they are carried ONLY when `op` is `write` or `suggest`. On `op: 'none'` the wire sentinels (`value: ""`, `reason: ""`) carry no information and are dropped. **`value` is kept even when it is the empty string** on a write/suggest - that is precisely the signal `schema.ts:215-219` erases, and Task 17 turns it into `empty_value_at_parse`. `reason` is kept only when non-blank (a blank reason is the same sentinel).

**TOTALITY IS NOT OPTIONAL.** `parseExtractionText` throws by design on malformed input, and the run log's `parse` failure kind is precisely when `parseExtractionOps` is handed unparseable text. A throwing helper would take down the recorder on the one failure the recorder exists to capture (spec 7.1).

```ts
// parseExtractionOps: the RAW per-target op view of rawText, before any folding
// or downgrading. Design 7.1 - the parsed ExtractionResult is unusable for this
// because it folds address/type sentinels to absent and rewrites a value-less
// write/suggest into { op: 'none' }, so a decline and a failure look identical.
// It MUST be total: parseExtractionText throws by design, and this is called on
// exactly the text that made it throw.
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { EMPTY_OPS_VIEW, parseExtractionOps } from '../src/services/extraction/schema.js';
import { DECISION_TARGETS } from '../src/services/extraction/runTypes.js';

    }

  });

  it('never leaks state between calls', () => {
    expect(parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"x"}}}').pets.op).toBe('write');
    expect(EMPTY_OPS_VIEW.pets).toEqual({ op: 'absent' });

    // into dropped/empty_value_at_parse.
```

```ts
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
// Raw per-target op view (AI run log, design 2026-08-06 section 7.1)
// ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

/** One target as the MODEL stated it, before any folding or downgrading. */
export interface ExtractionOpView {
  op: ProposedOp;
  /** Carried ONLY on write/suggest, and KEPT even when it is the empty string -
   *  an empty value on a write/suggest is exactly what parseExtractionText
   *  erases at :215-219, and it is what makes empty_value_at_parse detectable. */
  value?: string;
  /** Carried ONLY on write/suggest, and only when non-blank. */
  reason?: string;
}

export type ExtractionOpsView = Record<DecisionTarget, ExtractionOpView>;

function absentView(): ExtractionOpsView {
  const view = {} as ExtractionOpsView;
  for (const target of DECISION_TARGETS) view[target] = { op: 'absent' };
  return view;
}

/** All twelve targets absent. Frozen - callers must never mutate it. */
export const EMPTY_OPS_VIEW: ExtractionOpsView = Object.freeze(absentView());

/** The clamped reason, when the raw value is a non-blank string. */
function readReason(raw: unknown): string | undefined {
  return typeof raw === 'string' && raw.trim().length > 0 ? clamp(raw, MAX_REASON_CHARS) : undefined;
}

/**
 * The RAW per-target op view of the model's response text.
 *
 * WHY NOT the parsed ExtractionResult (design 7.1): parseExtractionText folds
 * the address and type `none` sentinels to ABSENT (:233, :258-263), so a
 * decline is indistinguishable from a silence; and it REWRITES a write/suggest
 * whose value is unusable into { op: 'none' } while dropping the reason
 * (:215-219), so a parsed `none` means EITHER "the model declined" OR "the
 * model tried and supplied an unusable value" - and the second is precisely
 * what QA needs to see, because it is the model FAILING rather than abstaining.
 *
 * TOTAL BY CONTRACT - it never throws. On unparseable or non-object input every
 * target comes back `absent`, and the recorder then records every target as
 * not_addressed - never no_finding, because a decline that was never observed
 * must never be inferred.
 */
export function parseExtractionOps(rawText: string | undefined): ExtractionOpsView {
  const view = absentView();
  if (rawText === undefined) return view;
  let parsed: unknown;
  try {
    parsed = JSON.parse(rawText);
  } catch {
    return view;
  }
  if (!isRecord(parsed)) return view;

  // The eight scalars: fields.<name>.op, verbatim.
  const rawFields = isRecord(parsed.fields) ? parsed.fields : {};
  for (const field of EXTRACTABLE_FIELDS) {
    const rawOp = rawFields[field];
    if (!isRecord(rawOp)) continue;
    const op = rawOp.op;
    if (op !== 'none' && op !== 'write' && op !== 'suggest') continue;
    if (op === 'none') {
      view[field] = { op }; // the value/reason sentinels carry no information
      continue;
    }
    view[field] = {
      op,
      ...(typeof rawOp.value === 'string' && { value: rawOp.value }),
      ...(readReason(rawOp.reason) !== undefined && { reason: readReason(rawOp.reason) }),
    };
  }

  // address: the op only - no parts folding, so a `none` stays a `none`.
  const rawAddress = parsed.address;
  if (isRecord(rawAddress)) {
    const op = rawAddress.op;
    if (op === 'none') {
      view.address = { op };
    } else if (op === 'write' || op === 'suggest') {
      view.address = {
        op,
        ...(readReason(rawAddress.reason) !== undefined && { reason: readReason(rawAddress.reason) }),
      };
    }
  }

  // status: the boolean IS the op.
  const rawStatus = parsed.statusAdvance;
  if (isRecord(rawStatus) && typeof rawStatus.suggest === 'boolean') {
    view.status = rawStatus.suggest
      ? { op: 'suggest', ...(readReason(rawStatus.reason) !== undefined && { reason: readReason(rawStatus.reason) }) }
      : { op: 'none' };
  }

  // type: "none" is the decline sentinel. ANY other non-empty string is the
  // model proposing something - including an off-enum value, which is a FAILED
  // ATTEMPT and must never read as a decline.
  const rawType = parsed.typeSuggestion;
  if (isRecord(rawType) && typeof rawType.value === 'string' && rawType.value.length > 0) {
    view.type =
      rawType.value === 'none'
        ? { op: 'none' }
        : {
            op: 'suggest',
            value: rawType.value,
            ...(readReason(rawType.reason) !== undefined && { reason: readReason(rawType.reason) }),
          };
  }

  // phone: the empty-string phone is the decline sentinel.
  const rawPhone = parsed.phoneAddition;
  if (isRecord(rawPhone) && typeof rawPhone.phone === 'string') {
    view.phone =
      rawPhone.phone.trim().length === 0
        ? { op: 'none' }
        : {
            op: 'suggest',
            value: rawPhone.phone,
            ...(readReason(rawPhone.reason) !== undefined && { reason: readReason(rawPhone.reason) }),
          };
  }

  return view;
}
```
