# SLICE 8 - The window builders

Spec 6.3 (as amended) splits this in two. A SKIP records a **light** window - `cursor`, `newestTsMsgId`, `windowCappedAtLimit`, and the message ids with their `type`/`direction` - but NO capping, NO `chars`, NO `hash`. Capping and hashing happen after the freshness gate, and `no_new_client` is the common steady-state outcome, so doing that work purely to log it would put it on the hot path. **The light window answers the question completely: which messages existed, and where the cursor sat. Bytes only matter when bytes were sent.** A run that actually calls the model records a **full** window.

### Task 16: Export the four job helpers; build `runWindow.ts`

**Files:** Modify `app/src/jobs/extraction.ts` (add `export` to four declarations); create `app/src/services/extraction/runWindow.ts`, `app/test/extractionRunWindow.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `renderUtteranceLine` (Task 6), `RunWindow*` (Task 2), `TranscriptUtterance`, and the job's already-exported constants `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES` (`app/src/jobs/extraction.ts:38`), `MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS` (`:40`), `NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP` (`:50`), `SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP` (`:52`), `WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET` (`:54`), `TRUNCATION_MARKER` (`:56`).
- Produces: `LightWindowMessage`, `WindowMessagePieces`, `BuildLightRunWindowInput`, `BuildFullRunWindowInput`, `buildLightRunWindow`, `buildFullRunWindow`, `hashRenderedMessage`. Consumed by Task 19.

**The hash join is exact and non-negotiable** (spec 6.1): a message's utterances are joined by a SINGLE `\n`, in the order `toUtterances` produced them, with NO trailing newline, each rendered through `renderUtteranceLine`; sha256, first 16 hex.

**Why exporting the four helpers matters:** reimplementing any of `toUtterances`, `capUtterances`, `clampHeadTail`, or `toProfile` would guarantee the hash mismatch this field exists to detect (spec 6.4).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

Create `app/test/extractionRunWindow.test.ts`. The round-trip test is the load-bearing one, and it uses a MULTI-MESSAGE fixture INCLUDING A CALL on purpose: `buildExtractionUserContent` sorts globally by `at`, and every line of a call transcript shares the call row's timestamp (`app/src/jobs/extraction.ts:132`), so a single-message fixture structurally cannot detect a divergence between the per-message hash and the request bytes.

```ts
// Window fidelity (design 6.1, 6.3 as amended). Two shapes: LIGHT for skips,
// FULL once the model is actually going to be called.
import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { buildExtractionUserContent, renderUtteranceLine } from '../src/services/extraction/prompt.js';
import {
  buildFullRunWindow,
  buildLightRunWindow,
  hashRenderedMessage,
} from '../src/services/extraction/runWindow.js';
import {
  MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS,
  MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES,
  NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
  SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
  TRUNCATION_MARKER,
  WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET,
} from '../src/jobs/extraction.js';
import type { TranscriptUtterance } from '../src/adapters/extraction.js';

const SMS_ID = '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z#s1';
const CALL_ID = '2026-08-06T10:05:00.000Z#c2';
const EMAIL_ID = '2026-08-06T10:09:00.000Z#e3';

function utt(tsMsgId: string, at: string, text: string, over: Partial<TranscriptUtterance> = {}): TranscriptUtterance {
  return { tsMsgId, speaker: 'client', text, at, channel: 'sms', ...over };
}

/** A three-message window whose MIDDLE message is a two-line call - so every
 *  call line shares one timestamp and the global sort must interleave nothing. */
function multiMessagePieces() {
  const sms = [utt(SMS_ID, '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'hello there')];
  const call = [
    utt(CALL_ID, '2026-08-06T10:05:00.000Z', 'how can I help', { speaker: 'staff', channel: 'voice' }),
    utt(CALL_ID, '2026-08-06T10:05:00.000Z', 'I need a 2 bedroom', { channel: 'voice' }),
  ];
  const email = [utt(EMAIL_ID, '2026-08-06T10:09:00.000Z', 'sending my docs', { channel: 'email' })];
  return [
    { tsMsgId: SMS_ID, type: 'sms', direction: 'inbound', raw: sms, capped: sms, capChars: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP },
    { tsMsgId: CALL_ID, type: 'call', direction: 'inbound', raw: call, capped: call, capChars: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP },
    { tsMsgId: EMAIL_ID, type: 'email', direction: 'inbound', raw: email, capped: email, capChars: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP },
  ];
}

describe('hashRenderedMessage', () => {
  it('is sha256 first-16-hex over renderUtteranceLine output joined by ONE newline', () => {
    const a = utt(SMS_ID, '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'line a');
    const b = utt(SMS_ID, '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'line b');
    expect(hashRenderedMessage([a, b])).toBe(
      createHash('sha256')
        .update([renderUtteranceLine(a), renderUtteranceLine(b)].join('\n'), 'utf8')
        .digest('hex')
        .slice(0, 16),
    );
    expect(hashRenderedMessage([a, b])).toHaveLength(16);
  });

  it('ROUND TRIP over a MULTI-MESSAGE window including a call: every hash matches the bytes the request carried', () => {
    // This is the whole point, and it needs several messages: the request is
    // built by a GLOBAL sort over all utterances, and every line of a call
    // shares the call row's timestamp (jobs/extraction.ts:132). A single-message
    // fixture cannot detect a divergence from that sort.
    //
    // A naive \n-join of the RAW utterance texts would also hash bytes that were
    // never sent (no speaker prefix, no single-line normalization) and produce a
    // permanent false mismatch on every run.
    const pieces = multiMessagePieces();
    const transcript = pieces.flatMap((p) => p.capped);
    const user = buildExtractionUserContent({
      profile: { contactType: 'tenant', phones: [] },
      transcript,
    });
    const body = user.slice(user.indexOf('TRANSCRIPT\n') + 'TRANSCRIPT\n'.length);
    const renderedLines = body.split('\n');

    // Each message's rendered lines must be CONTIGUOUS in the request, in the
    // order toUtterances produced them - and hashing that slice must match.
    let cursor = 0;
    for (const p of pieces) {
      const slice = renderedLines.slice(cursor, cursor + p.capped.length);
      expect(slice).toEqual(p.capped.map(renderUtteranceLine));
      expect(hashRenderedMessage(p.capped)).toBe(
        createHash('sha256').update(slice.join('\n'), 'utf8').digest('hex').slice(0, 16),
      );
      cursor += p.capped.length;
    }
    expect(cursor).toBe(renderedLines.length);
  });

  it('the stored hashes equal the ones buildFullRunWindow records for the same pieces', () => {
    const pieces = multiMessagePieces();
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: '',
      fetchedCount: 3,
      agedOutTsMsgIds: [],
      perMessage: pieces,
      included: new Set([SMS_ID, CALL_ID, EMAIL_ID]),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
      newestTsMsgId: EMAIL_ID,
    });
    expect(w.messages.map((m) => m.hash)).toEqual(pieces.map((p) => hashRenderedMessage(p.capped)));
  });
});

describe('buildLightRunWindow - what a SKIP records', () => {
  it('records ids, type, direction, tier, cursor and the fetch cap - and NOTHING byte-shaped', () => {
    const w = buildLightRunWindow({
      cursor: SMS_ID,
      fetchedCount: 2,
      agedOutTsMsgIds: ['2026-07-01T00:00:00.000Z#s0'],
      messages: [
        { tsMsgId: SMS_ID, type: 'sms', direction: 'inbound' },
        { tsMsgId: CALL_ID, type: 'call', direction: 'inbound' },
      ],
      newestTsMsgId: CALL_ID,
    });
    expect(w.detail).toBe('light');
    expect(w.cursor).toBe(SMS_ID);
    expect(w.newestTsMsgId).toBe(CALL_ID);
    expect(w.windowCappedAtLimit).toBe(false);
    expect(w.messages).toEqual([
      { tsMsgId: SMS_ID, type: 'sms', direction: 'inbound', tier: 'seen' },
      { tsMsgId: CALL_ID, type: 'call', direction: 'inbound', tier: 'new' },
    ]);
    // Age exclusions are free (a filter); char_budget needs capping, so a light
    // window never has one.
    expect(w.excluded).toEqual([{ tsMsgId: '2026-07-01T00:00:00.000Z#s0', cause: 'age_30d' }]);
    expect(w.totalChars).toBeUndefined();
    expect(w.windowParams).toBeUndefined();
    expect(w.noContent).toBeUndefined();
    expect(w.hasInferredRoleContent).toBeUndefined();
    expect(w.messages.every((m) => m.hash === undefined && m.chars === undefined)).toBe(true);
  });

  it('flags windowCappedAtLimit ONLY when the fetch returned exactly the cap', () => {
    const base = { cursor: '', agedOutTsMsgIds: [], messages: [] };
    expect(buildLightRunWindow({ ...base, fetchedCount: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES }).windowCappedAtLimit).toBe(true);
    expect(buildLightRunWindow({ ...base, fetchedCount: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES - 1 }).windowCappedAtLimit).toBe(false);
  });

  it('produces an honest empty window', () => {
    const w = buildLightRunWindow({ cursor: 'c', fetchedCount: 0, agedOutTsMsgIds: [], messages: [] });
    expect(w).toMatchObject({ detail: 'light', cursor: 'c', messages: [], excluded: [], windowCappedAtLimit: false });
    expect(w.newestTsMsgId).toBeUndefined();
  });
});

describe('buildFullRunWindow', () => {
  it('records the byte-affecting constants so a constant change is distinguishable from a code change', () => {
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: '', fetchedCount: 3, agedOutTsMsgIds: [],
      perMessage: multiMessagePieces(),
      included: new Set([SMS_ID, CALL_ID, EMAIL_ID]),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
    });
    expect(w.detail).toBe('full');
    expect(w.windowParams).toEqual({
      newMessageCharCap: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
      seenMessageCharCap: SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
      windowCharBudget: WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET,
      maxTranscriptMessages: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES,
      maxTranscriptAgeDays: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS,
      truncationMarker: TRUNCATION_MARKER,
    });
  });

  it('tags tier and the EXACT capChars this run applied, not just the label', () => {
    const [sms, call] = multiMessagePieces();
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: SMS_ID, fetchedCount: 2, agedOutTsMsgIds: [],
      perMessage: [{ ...sms!, capChars: SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP }, call!],
      included: new Set([SMS_ID, CALL_ID]),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
    });
    expect(w.messages.map((m) => [m.tier, m.capChars])).toEqual([
      ['seen', SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP],
      ['new', NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP],
    ]);
  });

  it('marks truncated when capping actually shortened the message', () => {
    const long = utt(SMS_ID, '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'x'.repeat(SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP + 100));
    const clipped = utt(SMS_ID, '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'x'.repeat(SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP));
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: 'zzz', fetchedCount: 1, agedOutTsMsgIds: [],
      perMessage: [{ tsMsgId: SMS_ID, type: 'sms', direction: 'inbound', raw: [long], capped: [clipped], capChars: SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP }],
      included: new Set([SMS_ID]),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
    });
    expect(w.messages[0]!.truncated).toBe(true);
    expect(w.messages[0]!.chars).toBe(SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP);
  });

  it('separates the two exclusion causes and never conflates them', () => {
    const [sms, call] = multiMessagePieces();
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: '', fetchedCount: 3, agedOutTsMsgIds: ['old#1'],
      perMessage: [sms!, call!],
      included: new Set([CALL_ID]),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
    });
    expect(w.excluded).toEqual([
      { tsMsgId: 'old#1', cause: 'age_30d' },
      { tsMsgId: SMS_ID, cause: 'char_budget' },
    ]);
  });

  it('records a zero-utterance message as noContent and NEVER as read', () => {
    // In practice a call whose transcript is not `completed`. It still enters
    // the included set with size 0; recording it as READ would be a false claim
    // that the model saw a call it never saw (design 6.1).
    const [sms] = multiMessagePieces();
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: '', fetchedCount: 2, agedOutTsMsgIds: [],
      perMessage: [
        { tsMsgId: CALL_ID, type: 'call', direction: 'inbound', raw: [], capped: [], capChars: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP },
        sms!,
      ],
      included: new Set([CALL_ID, SMS_ID]),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
    });
    expect(w.noContent).toEqual([CALL_ID]);
    expect(w.messages.map((m) => m.tsMsgId)).toEqual([SMS_ID]);
    expect(w.excluded.some((e) => e.tsMsgId === CALL_ID)).toBe(false);
  });

  it('totalChars sums only the messages that actually reached the model', () => {
    const w = buildFullRunWindow({
      cursor: '', fetchedCount: 2, agedOutTsMsgIds: [],
      perMessage: [
        { tsMsgId: 'a#1', type: 'sms', direction: 'inbound', raw: [utt('a#1', '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'aaaa')], capped: [utt('a#1', '2026-08-06T10:00:00.000Z', 'aaaa')], capChars: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP },
        { tsMsgId: 'b#1', type: 'sms', direction: 'inbound', raw: [utt('b#1', '2026-08-06T10:01:00.000Z', 'bb')], capped: [utt('b#1', '2026-08-06T10:01:00.000Z', 'bb')], capChars: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP },
      ],
      included: new Set(['b#1']),
      hasInferredRoleContent: false,
    });
    expect(w.totalChars).toBe(2);
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Write `runWindow.ts`**

```ts
// The run log's window block (design 2026-08-06 sections 6.1 and 6.3).
//
// Stores message IDS, not message text - text is rehydrated at view time. The
// per-message `hash` is produced by renderUtteranceLine, THE SAME function that
// builds the request, so the recorded hash and the sent bytes can never
// diverge. It is NOT a \n-join of the raw utterance texts: the real renderer
// applies single-line normalization and speaker prefixes, so a naive join would
// hash bytes that were never sent and produce a permanent false mismatch on
// every run.
//
// TWO SHAPES. A SKIP records a LIGHT window - which messages existed and where
// the cursor sat - because capping and hashing happen AFTER the freshness gate
// and `no_new_client` is the common steady-state outcome; doing that work purely
// to log it would put it on the hot path. Bytes only matter when bytes were
// sent. A run that reaches the model records a FULL window.
//
// DRIFT DETECTION, NOT REPLAY. A change to toUtterances, capUtterances,
// clampHeadTail, or the renderer changes the bytes for the same stored message,
// so a mismatch means "something changed", not necessarily "the message
// changed". windowParams records the byte-affecting CONSTANTS, so a reader can
// tell a constant change from an algorithm change: if the params match and the
// hash does not, the code changed.
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { renderUtteranceLine } from './prompt.js';
import type { TranscriptUtterance } from '../../adapters/extraction.js';
import type { RunWindow, RunWindowExcluded, RunWindowMessage } from './runTypes.js';
import {
  MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS,
  MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES,
  NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
  SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
  TRUNCATION_MARKER,
  WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET,
} from '../../jobs/extraction.js';

/** The identity of one fetched message - all a LIGHT window needs. */
export interface LightWindowMessage {
  tsMsgId: string;
  /** MessageItem.type at run time. */
  type: string;
  /** MessageItem.direction at run time. */
  direction: string;
}

/** One message's pieces, as the job computed them for a FULL window. */
export interface WindowMessagePieces extends LightWindowMessage {
  /** Utterances BEFORE capping. EMPTY means the message carried nothing. */
  raw: TranscriptUtterance[];
  /** Utterances AFTER capping - the exact set that reached the model. */
  capped: TranscriptUtterance[];
  /** The exact per-message cap this run applied to this message. */
  capChars: number;
}

export interface BuildLightRunWindowInput {
  cursor: string;
  /** How many rows listByConversation returned (to detect the fetch cap). */
  fetchedCount: number;
  /** Fetched, then dropped by the MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS cutoff. */
  agedOutTsMsgIds: string[];
  /** Chronological, post-age-cutoff. */
  messages: LightWindowMessage[];
  newestTsMsgId?: string;
}

export interface BuildFullRunWindowInput extends Omit<BuildLightRunWindowInput, 'messages'> {
  /** Chronological, post-age-cutoff, in the job's own order. */
  perMessage: WindowMessagePieces[];
  /** tsMsgIds that survived the whole-window char budget. */
  included: Set<string>;
  hasInferredRoleContent: boolean;
}

/**
 * sha256, first 16 hex, of the EXACT rendered text this message contributed to
 * the user content.
 *
 * THE JOIN IS EXACT AND NON-NEGOTIABLE (design 6.1) - this single detail decides
 * whether every stored hash matches or every one fails: a SINGLE \n, in the
 * order toUtterances produced them (chronological, and for a call transcript,
 * line order), with NO trailing newline.
 */
export function hashRenderedMessage(utterances: TranscriptUtterance[]): string {
  return createHash('sha256')
    .update(utterances.map(renderUtteranceLine).join('\n'), 'utf8')
    .digest('hex')
    .slice(0, 16);
}

/** 'new' = newer than the cursor (this run is its one full-fidelity read). */
const tierOf = (tsMsgId: string, cursor: string): 'new' | 'seen' => (tsMsgId > cursor ? 'new' : 'seen');

/** Messages beyond MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES are never fetched, so they cannot be
 *  enumerated in `excluded` without a second read this design declines to pay
 *  for - an honest boolean beats implying the list is exhaustive (6.1). */
const cappedAtLimit = (fetchedCount: number): boolean => fetchedCount === MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES;

export function buildLightRunWindow(input: BuildLightRunWindowInput): RunWindow {
  return {
    detail: 'light',
    cursor: input.cursor,
    ...(input.newestTsMsgId !== undefined && { newestTsMsgId: input.newestTsMsgId }),
    windowCappedAtLimit: cappedAtLimit(input.fetchedCount),
    messages: input.messages.map((m) => ({ ...m, tier: tierOf(m.tsMsgId, input.cursor) })),
    // Age exclusions are free (a filter over what we already fetched).
    // char_budget exclusions require the budget fill, which requires capping -
    // so a light window never has one.
    excluded: input.agedOutTsMsgIds.map((tsMsgId) => ({ tsMsgId, cause: 'age_30d' as const })),
  };
}

export function buildFullRunWindow(input: BuildFullRunWindowInput): RunWindow {
  const messages: RunWindowMessage[] = [];
  const excluded: RunWindowExcluded[] = [];
  const noContent: string[] = [];
  let totalChars = 0;

  // Age exclusions first, so `excluded` reads oldest-cause-first.
  for (const tsMsgId of input.agedOutTsMsgIds) excluded.push({ tsMsgId, cause: 'age_30d' });

  for (const piece of input.perMessage) {
    if (piece.raw.length === 0) {
      // Zero utterances - the message carried NOTHING to the model. In practice
      // a call whose transcript is not `completed`. It still enters the included
      // set with size 0; recording it as READ would be a false claim that the
      // model saw a call it never saw.
      noContent.push(piece.tsMsgId);
      continue;
    }
    if (!input.included.has(piece.tsMsgId)) {
      excluded.push({ tsMsgId: piece.tsMsgId, cause: 'char_budget' });
      continue;
    }
    const rawChars = piece.raw.reduce((n, u) => n + u.text.length, 0);
    const chars = piece.capped.reduce((n, u) => n + u.text.length, 0);
    totalChars += chars;
    messages.push({
      tsMsgId: piece.tsMsgId,
      type: piece.type,
      direction: piece.direction,
      tier: tierOf(piece.tsMsgId, input.cursor),
      capChars: piece.capChars,
      truncated: rawChars > piece.capChars,
      chars,
      hash: hashRenderedMessage(piece.capped),
    });
  }

  return {
    detail: 'full',
    cursor: input.cursor,
    ...(input.newestTsMsgId !== undefined && { newestTsMsgId: input.newestTsMsgId }),
    hasInferredRoleContent: input.hasInferredRoleContent,
    totalChars,
    windowCappedAtLimit: cappedAtLimit(input.fetchedCount),
    windowParams: {
      newMessageCharCap: NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
      seenMessageCharCap: SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP,
      windowCharBudget: WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET,
      maxTranscriptMessages: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES,
      maxTranscriptAgeDays: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS,
      truncationMarker: TRUNCATION_MARKER,
    },
    messages,
    excluded,
    noContent,
  };
}
```

**IMPORT-CYCLE WATCH ITEM.** `runWindow.ts` imports the six constants from `jobs/extraction.ts`, and Task 19 makes `jobs/extraction.ts` import `buildLightRunWindow`/`buildFullRunWindow`. That is a cycle. ESM tolerates it for hoisted function declarations and `const` module constants read at CALL time, but it is fragile. **Preferred: do the safe move preemptively** - relocate the six constants into `runTypes.ts` and re-export them from `jobs/extraction.ts` (`export { NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP, ... } from '../services/extraction/runTypes.js';`) so `app/test/extractionJob.test.ts:16-24` and this task's test keep importing them from the job unchanged. If you skip the move and `npm run typecheck` or a test surfaces a TDZ / undefined-constant error, do it then.

# SLICE 9 - The decisions builder

### Task 17: `buildDecisions` - join the raw op view with apply's outcomes

**Files:** Create `app/src/services/extraction/decisions.ts`, `app/test/extractionDecisions.test.ts`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `ExtractionOpsView` / `EMPTY_OPS_VIEW` (Task 15), `ApplyDecision` (Task 14), `RunDecision` / `DECISION_TARGETS` / `Verdict` (Task 2).
- Produces:

```ts
export interface BuildDecisionsInput {
  ops: ExtractionOpsView;
  rawTextPresent: boolean;
  applyDecisions: ApplyDecision[];
  /** Called with the target when the join finds NO explanation for a
   *  write/suggest that produced nothing. A plan or apply GAP - never normal. */
  onUnexplained?: (target: DecisionTarget) => void;
}
export function buildDecisions(input: BuildDecisionsInput): Partial<Record<DecisionTarget, RunDecision>>;
```

Consumed by Task 19.

**Join rules, in order. Write them into the module doc comment.**

1. An apply outcome (`wrote` / `suggested` / `dropped`) wins outright, carrying `proposedValue`, `coercedValue`, `previousValue`, `reason`, `demotedFrom`, `dropReason` through.
2. Otherwise `proposedOp === 'none'` gives `no_finding` - the model explicitly declined.
3. Otherwise `proposedOp === 'absent'` gives `not_addressed` - never mentioned at all.
4. Otherwise the model proposed `write`/`suggest` and apply produced NOTHING. **`schema.ts` folded it away before apply ran** (spec 7.2, as amended). Two sub-cases:
   - The fold is EXPLAINED - a scalar whose raw `value` is empty or whitespace (`schema.ts:215-219`), or the `address` target at all (`schema.ts:258-263` drops a zero-usable-parts address, and the ops view carries no parts to check) - gives `dropped` / `empty_value_at_parse`. This is the model sending nothing usable, and it is deliberately DISTINCT from `invalid_value`, which is apply's `coerceField` rejecting a well-formed but out-of-range value: QA needs to tell those apart.
   - The fold is UNEXPLAINED - a non-empty value that apply nonetheless never reported. That is a GAP in Task 14's branch table or a new fold site, never a normal state. Record `dropped` with **NO `dropReason`** and call `onUnexplained(target)`. A reasonless drop renders as "unexplained" in the UI and warns in the log: **loud, not silently wrong.** Never guess a reason here.
5. When `rawTextPresent` is false, EVERY target is forced to `absent` before rules 2-4 run, so no target can be recorded `no_finding`. Asserted, not assumed - the spec does not permit inferring a decline that was never observed (spec 7.1).
6. Verdict is DERIVED, never passed in: `wrote` -> `auto_applied`; `suggested` -> `pending`; everything else -> `not_presented`.
7. All twelve targets always get a decision, so "the model never mentioned it" is answerable from the record alone.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test**

```ts
// Decision assembly (design 7.1). Sourced from rawText via parseExtractionOps,
// NEVER from the parsed result - the parsed result cannot distinguish a decline
// from a failure at any of the twelve targets.
import { describe, expect, it, vi } from 'vitest';
import { buildDecisions } from '../src/services/extraction/decisions.js';
import { EMPTY_OPS_VIEW, parseExtractionOps } from '../src/services/extraction/schema.js';
import { DECISION_TARGETS } from '../src/services/extraction/runTypes.js';

describe('buildDecisions', () => {
  it('covers all twelve targets, so "never mentioned" is answerable from the record alone', () => {
    const out = buildDecisions({ ops: EMPTY_OPS_VIEW, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [] });
    expect(Object.keys(out).sort()).toEqual([...DECISION_TARGETS].sort());
  });

  it('distinguishes an explicit decline (no_finding) from a silence (not_addressed)', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"none","value":"","reason":""}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [] });
    expect(out.pets).toEqual({ proposedOp: 'none', outcome: 'no_finding', verdict: 'not_presented' });
    expect(out.tenure).toEqual({ proposedOp: 'absent', outcome: 'not_addressed', verdict: 'not_presented' });
  });

  it('NEVER infers a decline when rawText was absent - only not_addressed', () => {
    // The console driver, or a driver failure before a response arrived.
    const out = buildDecisions({ ops: EMPTY_OPS_VIEW, rawTextPresent: false, applyDecisions: [] });
    for (const t of DECISION_TARGETS) expect(out[t]!.outcome).toBe('not_addressed');
  });

  it('records a write as wrote / auto_applied, carrying previousValue', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"two cats","reason":"said so"}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({
      ops, rawTextPresent: true,
      applyDecisions: [{
        target: 'pets', outcome: 'wrote',
        proposedValue: 'two cats', coercedValue: 'two cats',
        previousValue: 'a dog', reason: 'said so',
      }],
    });
    expect(out.pets).toEqual({
      proposedOp: 'write', proposedValue: 'two cats', coercedValue: 'two cats',
      previousValue: 'a dog', reason: 'said so',
      outcome: 'wrote', verdict: 'auto_applied',
    });
  });

  it('auto_applied does NOT imply the field was empty - previousValue tells them apart', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"two cats"}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({
      ops, rawTextPresent: true,
      applyDecisions: [{ target: 'pets', outcome: 'wrote', coercedValue: 'two cats' }],
    });
    expect(out.pets!.verdict).toBe('auto_applied');
    expect(out.pets!.previousValue).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it('records a suggestion as suggested / pending, preserving demotedFrom', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"two cats"}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({
      ops, rawTextPresent: true,
      applyDecisions: [{ target: 'pets', outcome: 'suggested', coercedValue: 'two cats', demotedFrom: 'write' }],
    });
    expect(out.pets).toMatchObject({
      proposedOp: 'write', outcome: 'suggested', demotedFrom: 'write', verdict: 'pending',
    });
  });

  it('carries every dropReason through as dropped / not_presented', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"phoneAddition":{"phone":"404-555-1212","label":"","reason":""}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({
      ops, rawTextPresent: true,
      applyDecisions: [{
        target: 'phone', outcome: 'dropped',
        dropReason: 'phone_owned_by_other', proposedValue: '404-555-1212',
      }],
    });
    expect(out.phone).toMatchObject({
      proposedOp: 'suggest', outcome: 'dropped',
      dropReason: 'phone_owned_by_other', verdict: 'not_presented',
    });
  });

  it('a scalar write with an EMPTY value reads as dropped/empty_value_at_parse, NEVER no_finding', () => {
    // schema.ts:215-219 rewrites a value-less write into { op: none } and drops
    // the reason, so apply never sees it and records nothing. The raw view still
    // says `write`, which is the model FAILING, not abstaining (7.1/7.2).
    const onUnexplained = vi.fn();
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"","reason":"unsure"}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [], onUnexplained });
    expect(out.pets).toMatchObject({
      proposedOp: 'write', outcome: 'dropped',
      dropReason: 'empty_value_at_parse', verdict: 'not_presented',
    });
    expect(onUnexplained).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it('empty_value_at_parse is DISTINCT from invalid_value', () => {
    // One is the model sending nothing usable; the other is apply's coerceField
    // rejecting something well-formed but wrong. QA needs to tell them apart.
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"voucherSize":{"op":"write","value":"99"}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({
      ops, rawTextPresent: true,
      applyDecisions: [{ target: 'voucherSize', outcome: 'dropped', dropReason: 'invalid_value', proposedValue: '99' }],
    });
    expect(out.voucherSize!.dropReason).toBe('invalid_value');
  });

  it('an address write the parse dropped for zero usable parts reads as empty_value_at_parse', () => {
    const onUnexplained = vi.fn();
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"address":{"op":"write","line1":"","city":"","reason":"heard one"}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [], onUnexplained });
    expect(out.address).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'dropped', dropReason: 'empty_value_at_parse' });
    expect(onUnexplained).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it('an address DECLINE still reads as no_finding even though parseExtractionText folds it away', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"address":{"op":"none","line1":"","reason":""}}');
    expect(buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [] }).address)
      .toMatchObject({ proposedOp: 'none', outcome: 'no_finding' });
  });

  it('a type decline reads as no_finding even though parseExtractionText folds it away', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"typeSuggestion":{"value":"none","reason":""}}');
    expect(buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [] }).type)
      .toMatchObject({ proposedOp: 'none', outcome: 'no_finding' });
  });

  it('an UNEXPLAINED gap is LOUD: a reasonless drop plus onUnexplained, never a guessed reason', () => {
    // A non-empty proposed value that apply nonetheless never reported means a
    // branch is missing from apply's decision table, or a new schema.ts fold
    // site exists. Recording a plausible-looking dropReason would hide it.
    const onUnexplained = vi.fn();
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"two cats"}}}');
    const out = buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [], onUnexplained });
    expect(out.pets).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'dropped', verdict: 'not_presented' });
    expect(out.pets!.dropReason).toBeUndefined();
    expect(onUnexplained).toHaveBeenCalledWith('pets');
  });

  it('is safe without an onUnexplained hook', () => {
    const ops = parseExtractionOps('{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"two cats"}}}');
    expect(() => buildDecisions({ ops, rawTextPresent: true, applyDecisions: [] })).not.toThrow();
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement**

```ts
// Decision assembly for the AI run log (design 2026-08-06 section 7.1).
//
// Decisions are built from what the model SAID (the raw op view of rawText),
// JOINED with apply's outcomes for what we DID. Two earlier revisions got this
// wrong in opposite directions - first deriving no_finding inside apply.ts
// (which fabricates evidence of evaluation, because apply's loop treats a
// missing field and an explicit op:'none' identically), then splitting the
// source by target (which still fabricates a decline whenever the downgrade at
// schema.ts:215-219 fires). Reading rawText for ALL TWELVE targets is both
// simpler and the only correct option.
//
// JOIN RULES:
//  1. An apply outcome (wrote | suggested | dropped) wins outright.
//  2. Else proposedOp 'none'   -> no_finding    (the model explicitly declined).
//  3. Else proposedOp 'absent' -> not_addressed (never mentioned at all).
//  4. Else the model proposed write/suggest and apply produced NOTHING, because
//     schema.ts folded it away before apply ran:
//       - EXPLAINED (an empty/whitespace scalar value, per schema.ts:215-219, or
//         the address target at all, per the zero-usable-parts fold at
//         schema.ts:258-263) -> dropped / empty_value_at_parse. Deliberately
//         DISTINCT from invalid_value: one is the model sending nothing usable,
//         the other is apply rejecting something well-formed but wrong.
//       - UNEXPLAINED (a non-empty value apply never reported) -> a GAP in
//         apply's decision table or a new fold site. Recorded as a REASONLESS
//         drop plus onUnexplained(). Loud, never a guessed reason.
//  5. rawText absent -> every target is forced to 'absent', so NO target can be
//     recorded no_finding. The spec does not permit inferring a decline that was
//     never observed.
//  6. Verdict is DERIVED here, never passed in.
import {
  DECISION_TARGETS,
  type DecisionTarget,
  type RunDecision,
  type Verdict,
} from './runTypes.js';
import type { ExtractionOpsView } from './schema.js';
import type { ApplyDecision } from './apply.js';

export interface BuildDecisionsInput {
  /** parseExtractionOps(rawText), or EMPTY_OPS_VIEW when no response arrived. */
  ops: ExtractionOpsView;
  /** False when the driver produced no rawText at all (the console driver, or a
   *  driver failure before a response arrived). */
  rawTextPresent: boolean;
  /** apply's per-target outcomes. Empty on a run that never reached apply. */
  applyDecisions: ApplyDecision[];
  /** Called when rule 4's UNEXPLAINED branch fires. Pass a logger warn. */
  onUnexplained?: (target: DecisionTarget) => void;
}

/** wrote -> auto_applied; suggested -> pending; everything else -> not_presented. */
function verdictFor(outcome: RunDecision['outcome']): Verdict {
  if (outcome === 'wrote') return 'auto_applied';
  if (outcome === 'suggested') return 'pending';
  return 'not_presented';
}

export function buildDecisions(input: BuildDecisionsInput): Partial<Record<DecisionTarget, RunDecision>> {
  const applied = new Map<string, ApplyDecision>();
  for (const d of input.applyDecisions) applied.set(d.target, d);

  const out: Partial<Record<DecisionTarget, RunDecision>> = {};
  for (const target of DECISION_TARGETS) {
    const op = input.ops[target];
    // Rule 5, asserted rather than assumed.
    const proposedOp = input.rawTextPresent ? op.op : 'absent';
    const hit = applied.get(target);

    let outcome: RunDecision['outcome'];
    let dropReason: RunDecision['dropReason'];
    if (hit !== undefined) {
      outcome = hit.outcome;
      dropReason = hit.dropReason;
    } else if (proposedOp === 'none') {
      outcome = 'no_finding';
    } else if (proposedOp === 'absent') {
      outcome = 'not_addressed';
    } else {
      outcome = 'dropped';
      // Rule 4. `address` carries no value in the ops view, and its only way to
      // vanish before apply is the zero-usable-parts fold - so it is always the
      // explained case. A scalar is explained only when its value was empty.
      const emptyValue = op.value === undefined || op.value.trim().length === 0;
      if (target === 'address' || emptyValue) {
        dropReason = 'empty_value_at_parse';
      } else {
        input.onUnexplained?.(target); // LOUD - never guess a reason
      }
    }

    const proposedValue =
      hit?.proposedValue ?? (proposedOp === 'write' || proposedOp === 'suggest' ? op.value : undefined);
    const reason = hit?.reason ?? op.reason;

    out[target] = {
      proposedOp,
      ...(proposedValue !== undefined && { proposedValue }),
      ...(hit?.coercedValue !== undefined && { coercedValue: hit.coercedValue }),
      ...(hit?.previousValue !== undefined && { previousValue: hit.previousValue }),
      ...(reason !== undefined && { reason }),
      ...(hit?.demotedFrom !== undefined && { demotedFrom: hit.demotedFrom }),
      outcome,
      ...(dropReason !== undefined && { dropReason }),
      verdict: verdictFor(outcome),
    };
  }
  return out;
}
```

**Import-direction watch item:** `decisions.ts` imports `ApplyDecision` from `apply.ts` TYPE-ONLY (erased at runtime), and `apply.ts` imports nothing from `decisions.ts`. That direction is fixed - never reverse it, or `apply.ts` gains a path toward `ai_runs`.

# SLICE 10 - `processRow` fills a caller-owned `RunDraft`; one writer, one write

Read spec section 8 in full before starting. Four properties are non-negotiable, each with its own test:

1. **The envelope has exactly ONE writer and writes EXACTLY ONCE per run, on every terminal path.** `runDueExtractions` OWNS the draft, hands it to `processRow` to fill, and performs the single write.
2. **The draft is caller-owned precisely so the backstop can still write it.** If `processRow` returned the draft, an unexpected throw would lose it - and with it the window, the decisions, the raw text, and (worse) any `runId` already stamped onto suggestion rows by `applyExtraction`, orphaning them permanently. The caller allocates `runId` and `startedAt` before the call; `processRow` mutates in place.
3. **The outer try/catch in `runDueExtractions` STAYS, as a backstop.** It is NOT replaced by the returned outcome. It provides per-row batch isolation (`app/src/jobs/extraction.ts:401-425` at HEAD): `processRow` not throwing on KNOWN failures does not mean it can never throw - an unhandled defect, an OOM, or a repo path nobody enumerated would otherwise abort the entire poll batch. **The catch must still call `repo.fail`.**
4. **A skip-path `complete()` throw is a FAILURE, not a skip.** All four `repo.complete()` call sites are `error.kind: 'complete'` with `outcome: 'failed'` and go through normal failure handling. Reading "it was a skip, so record `skipped`" is a silent regression: `complete()` is what clears the claim, so a throw there leaves the row CLAIMED and out of `byDueAt`, and recording it as a skip means no `repo.fail()` - hence no backoff, no attempt increment, and no park. The row would never run again and nothing would say so.

**Two branches are NOT recorded at all** (spec 6.3): a lost claim (the sliding debounce working as designed - recording it would be pure noise) and the defensive `listedDueAt === undefined` return (unreachable in practice; no meaningful `skipReason`).

**LINE ANCHORS: this slice restructures `jobs/extraction.ts`, so every reference below is by SYMBOL, not line number.** Anchor edits on `ExtractionJobDeps`, `processRow`, `runDueExtractions`, `toUtterances`, `capUtterances`, `toProfile`, and the four `repo.complete(` call sites. Do not trust any `jobs/extraction.ts:NNN` from an earlier slice once you are inside this one.

### Task 18: `ExtractionJobDeps` gains a REQUIRED `aiRuns` and `now()`, at all three sites

**Files:** Modify the `ExtractionJobDeps` interface in `app/src/jobs/extraction.ts`; `app/src/worker.ts` (the `config.aiExtractionEnabled` block, `:316-357`); `app/src/routes/dev.ts` (the `extractionDeps` factory, `:339-370`); the `deps` builder in `makeHarness` (`app/test/extractionJob.test.ts:206-215`)

**Interfaces:** Produces

```ts
  aiRuns: Pick<AiRunsRepo, 'putRun' | 'setVerdict'>;
  now(): string;
```

Both **REQUIRED**, so a missed construction site is a typecheck failure rather than a silently empty log - spec 8 names exactly that risk: "Missing the dev tick leaves the log permanently empty in e2e and local development - the exact place this feature is first exercised."

**Why `now()` is injected and separate from `nowIso`** (spec section 6, as amended): the run record's `startedAt`/`finishedAt`/`durationMs` are REAL WALL-CLOCK times. The dev tick calls the job with a SIMULATED FUTURE clock - `app/src/routes/dev.ts:373` passes `Date.now() + config.aiExtractionDebounceMs + 1000` so a just-scheduled row is already past its sliding `dueAt` - so using the poll clock would stamp every dev and e2e run about one debounce into the future and mis-sort the log against real time. **Domain logic (cursor comparisons, the age cutoff, `repo.claim`/`complete`/`fail`) keeps using `nowIso`, unchanged.** Only the record's timestamps use `now()`. Injected so tests can pin it.

```ts
describe('runDueExtractions - the run log envelope', () => {
  const WROTE_PETS = 'EXTRACT:{"fields":{"pets":{"op":"write","value":"two cats","reason":"said so"}}}';

  it('writes EXACTLY ONE record per run on the success path', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()],
      messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', WROTE_PETS)],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.aiRuns.putRun).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'applied', trigger: 'sms', driver: 'fake' });
    expect(h.runs[0]!.decisions['pets']).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'wrote', verdict: 'auto_applied' });
  });

  it('stamps REAL WALL-CLOCK timestamps from now(), never the simulated poll clock', () => {
    // The dev tick advances nowIso by the debounce window, so the poll clock
    // would stamp dev and e2e runs into the future (design section 6, amended).
    return (async () => {
      const h = makeHarness({
        dueRows: [dueRow()],
        messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', WROTE_PETS)],
        contact: tenantContact(),
        conversation: convWith('c1'),
      });
      await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
      expect(h.runs[0]!.startedAt).toBe(WALL_NOW);
      expect(h.runs[0]!.startedAt).not.toBe(NOW);
      expect(h.runs[0]!.finishedAt).toBe(WALL_NOW); // the pinned clock does not advance
      expect(h.runs[0]!.durationMs).toBe(0);
    })();
  });

  it('records a run of pure DROPS as no_op, not applied', async () => {
    // A run that called the model and wrote/suggested/noted NOTHING is a no_op,
    // even when every finding was dropped (design section 6).
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()],
      messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', 'EXTRACT:{"fields":{"voucherSize":{"op":"write","value":"2"}}}')],
      contact: unknownContact(), // voucherSize does not apply -> wrong_contact_type
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.runs[0]!.outcome).toBe('no_op');
    expect(h.runs[0]!.decisions['voucherSize']).toMatchObject({
      outcome: 'dropped', dropReason: 'wrong_contact_type', verdict: 'not_presented',
    });
  });

  it('records a no_new_client SKIP with a LIGHT window - ids and cursor, no bytes', async () => {
    // Capping and hashing happen AFTER the gate, and no_new_client is the common
    // steady-state outcome; doing that work purely to log it would put it on the
    // hot path. The light window answers the question completely (6.3, amended).
    const seen = msg(10, 'inbound', 'older');
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow({ cursor: seen.tsMsgId })],
      messages: [seen],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    const w = h.runs[0]!.window!;
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'skipped', skipReason: 'no_new_client' });
    expect(w.detail).toBe('light');
    expect(w.cursor).toBe(seen.tsMsgId);
    expect(w.messages.map((m) => m.tsMsgId)).toEqual([seen.tsMsgId]);
    expect(w.messages[0]!.hash).toBeUndefined();
    expect(w.messages[0]!.chars).toBeUndefined();
    expect(w.windowParams).toBeUndefined();
    expect(h.runs[0]!.decisions).toEqual({});
    // And the model was never called.
    expect(h.seen).toHaveLength(0);
  });

  it('records a FULL window - hashes, caps, params - once the model is called', async () => {
    const sms = msg(10, 'inbound', WROTE_PETS);
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()],
      messages: [sms],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    const w = h.runs[0]!.window!;
    expect(w.detail).toBe('full');
    expect(w.windowParams).toBeDefined();
    expect(w.messages[0]).toMatchObject({ tsMsgId: sms.tsMsgId, tier: 'new', truncated: false });
    expect(w.messages[0]!.hash).toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{16}$/);
    expect(w.totalChars).toBeGreaterThan(0);
  });

  it('records no_contact and ineligible_type with NO window (they exit before the fetch)', async () => {
    const noContact = makeHarness({ dueRows: [dueRow()], conversation: convWith('c1'), contact: undefined });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, noContact.deps);
    expect(noContact.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'skipped', skipReason: 'no_contact' });
    expect(noContact.runs[0]!.window).toBeUndefined();

    const landlord = makeHarness({ dueRows: [dueRow()], conversation: convWith('c1'), contact: landlordContact() });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, landlord.deps);
    expect(landlord.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'skipped', skipReason: 'ineligible_type' });
    expect(landlord.runs[0]!.window).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it('records empty_window when nothing survived the cutoffs', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow({ channel: 'voice' })], // voice bypasses the freshness gate
      messages: [],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'skipped', skipReason: 'empty_window' });
    expect(h.runs[0]!.window!.detail).toBe('light');
  });

  it('records NOTHING for a lost claim - the sliding debounce is not a run', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()], claimResult: false,
      contact: tenantContact(), conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.aiRuns.putRun).not.toHaveBeenCalled();
  });

  it('names the failing stage: refusal / parse / driver, and still records window + decisions', async () => {
    for (const failure of ['refusal', 'parse', 'driver'] as const) {
      const h = makeHarness({
        dueRows: [dueRow()],
        messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', 'hello')],
        contact: tenantContact(),
        conversation: convWith('c1'),
        driver: {
          kind: 'fake',
          extract: async () => ({
            ok: false,
            meta: { driver: 'fake', rawText: '{broken' },
            failure,
            message: `${failure} boom`,
          }),
        },
      });
      await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
      expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'failed', error: { kind: failure } });
      // A failed run STILL records window and decisions (design 6.3).
      expect(h.runs[0]!.window!.detail).toBe('full');
      expect(Object.keys(h.runs[0]!.decisions)).toHaveLength(12);
      // rawText survives a parse failure - the one case where it IS the answer.
      expect(h.runs[0]!.rawText).toBe('{broken');
    }
  });

  it('names error.kind repo when a WRAPPED repository read throws', async () => {
    // processRow wraps this site, so it takes the ordinary failed() path and the
    // backstop never runs. Task 20's backstop tests deliberately use an
    // UNWRAPPED site instead; the `window` assertion below is what distinguishes
    // the two states, so neither test can pass for the other's reason.
    const h = makeHarness({ dueRows: [dueRow()], contact: tenantContact(), conversation: convWith('c1') });
    (h.deps.messages.listByConversation as ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>).mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('ddb down'));
    const out = await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(out).toEqual({ processed: 0, failed: 1 });
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'failed', error: { kind: 'repo', message: 'ddb down' } });
    // It returned BEFORE the window was built - a state the backstop tests can
    // never produce, since they throw after the full window and rawText exist.
    expect(h.runs[0]!.window).toBeUndefined();
  });

  it('a SKIP-path complete() throw is failed/complete, keeps the skipReason, and still calls repo.fail', async () => {
    // Recording it as `skipped` would mean no repo.fail: no backoff, no attempt
    // increment, no park - the row stays CLAIMED and out of byDueAt and would
    // never run again, with nothing saying so (design 8).
    const seen = msg(10, 'inbound', 'older');
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow({ cursor: seen.tsMsgId })],
      messages: [seen],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    (h.repo.complete as ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>).mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('complete boom'));
    const out = await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(out).toEqual({ processed: 0, failed: 1 });
    expect(h.repo.fail).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({
      outcome: 'failed', skipReason: 'no_new_client', error: { kind: 'complete' },
    });
  });

  it('a SUCCESS-path complete() throw still records the run that mutated the contact', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()],
      messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', WROTE_PETS)],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    (h.repo.complete as ReturnType<typeof vi.fn>).mockRejectedValueOnce(new Error('complete boom'));
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.aiRuns.putRun).toHaveBeenCalledTimes(1);
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'failed', error: { kind: 'complete' } });
    expect(h.runs[0]!.decisions['pets']).toMatchObject({ outcome: 'wrote' });
  });

  it('stamps attempts and parked onto error - only runDueExtractions knows them', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow({ attempts: 4 })], // the next attempt reaches MAX -> PARK
      messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', 'hello')],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
      driver: {
        kind: 'fake',
        extract: async () => ({ ok: false, meta: { driver: 'fake' }, failure: 'driver', message: 'boom' }),
      },
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.runs[0]!.error).toEqual({ kind: 'driver', message: 'boom', attempts: 4, parked: true });
  });

  it('records driver, model, usage and promptFingerprint from the call meta', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()],
      messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', 'hello')],
      contact: tenantContact(),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
      driver: {
        kind: 'anthropic',
        extract: async () => ({
          ok: true,
          meta: {
            driver: 'anthropic',
            model: 'claude-opus-4-8',
            rawText: '{"fields":{}}',
            usage: { inputTokens: 100, outputTokens: 20 },
            promptFingerprint: 'abc123def456',
          },
          result: { fields: {} },
        }),
      },
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    expect(h.runs[0]).toMatchObject({
      driver: 'anthropic', model: 'claude-opus-4-8',
      usage: { inputTokens: 100, outputTokens: 20 },
      promptFingerprint: 'abc123def456', rawText: '{"fields":{}}',
    });
  });

  it('records profileFieldsPopulated - names only, never a profile snapshot', async () => {
    const h = makeHarness({
      dueRows: [dueRow()],
      messages: [msg(10, 'inbound', 'hello')],
      contact: tenantContactWith({ firstName: 'Ann', voucherSize: 2 }),
      conversation: convWith('c1'),
    });
    await runDueExtractions(NOW, h.deps);
    const fields = h.runs[0]!.profileFieldsPopulated!;
    expect(fields).toContain('firstName');
    expect(fields).toContain('voucherSize');
    expect(fields.join(',')).not.toContain('Ann');
  });
});
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add the draft types ABOVE `processRow`**

These must be declared BEFORE any code that reads them, so the Task 19/20 seam typechecks at every point:

```ts
/**
 * The run record UNDER CONSTRUCTION.
 *
 * OWNED BY THE CALLER, mutated by processRow. That ownership is the point: if
 * processRow RETURNED the draft, an unexpected throw would lose the window, the
 * decisions, the raw text, and - worst - any runId applyExtraction already
 * stamped onto suggestion rows, orphaning them permanently. The caller allocates
 * runId and startedAt first, so its backstop can always write what was built.
 *
 * A DRAFT NEVER TOUCHES A LOGGER. That is the decisive reason it is not thrown:
 * rawText, previousValue and proposedValue are all PII, and a thrown Error is
 * the one object type this codebase's logging conventions serialize WHOLESALE
 * (`logger.error({ err })`) (design section 8).
 */
export interface RunDraft {
  runId: string;
  /** REAL wall clock from deps.now() - never the poll's nowIso (design 6). */
  startedAt: string;
  conversationId: string;
  contactId?: string;
  trigger: RunTrigger;
  skipReason?: SkipReason;
  /** attempts/parked are filled by runDueExtractions - only IT knows them. */
  error?: { kind: RunErrorKind; message: string; attempts?: number; parked?: boolean };
  driver?: 'anthropic' | 'console' | 'fake';
  model?: string;
  promptFingerprint?: string;
  usage?: { inputTokens: number; outputTokens: number };
  window?: RunWindow;
  profileFieldsPopulated?: string[];
  rawText?: string;
  rawResult?: ExtractionResult;
  decisions?: Partial<Record<DecisionTarget, RunDecision>>;
  notedLines?: number;
  /** Earlier runs whose pending suggestion this run displaced (design 7.3). */
  displaced: Array<{ target: string; runId: string }>;
}

/** Allocate a draft BEFORE processRow runs, so the backstop can still write it. */
export function newRunDraft(row: DueExtractionItem, startedAt: string): RunDraft {
  return {
    runId: randomUUID(),
    startedAt,
    conversationId: row.conversationId,
    trigger: row.channel,
    displaced: [],
  };
}

/**
 * `record: false` is the ONLY non-recorded shape, and covers exactly the two
 * branches design 6.3 excludes: a lost claim (the sliding debounce working as
 * designed - recording it would be pure noise) and the defensive
 * `listedDueAt === undefined` return (unreachable in practice, no meaningful
 * skipReason).
 */
export type ProcessRowResult = { record: false } | { record: true; outcome: RunOutcome };
```

Add `import { randomUUID } from 'node:crypto';`; type imports `RunErrorKind`, `RunOutcome`, `RunTrigger`, `SkipReason`, `AiRunRecordInput`, `AiRunsRepo` from `../repos/aiRunsRepo.js`; `DecisionTarget`, `RunDecision`, `RunWindow`, `isDecisionTarget` from `../services/extraction/runTypes.js`; `buildFullRunWindow`, `buildLightRunWindow`, `type WindowMessagePieces` from `../services/extraction/runWindow.js`; `buildDecisions` from `../services/extraction/decisions.js`; `parseExtractionOps` from `../services/extraction/schema.js`; `type ExtractionResult` from `../adapters/extraction.js`.

Add this helper next to `toProfile`:

```ts
/**
 * The populated field NAMES on the profile the model reconciled against - NOT a
 * wholesale snapshot (design 6.2). Answers "it already had a last name and
 * proposed one anyway" without duplicating the record.
 */
function profileFieldNames(profile: ExtractionProfileSnapshot): string[] {
  const names = Object.entries(profile)
    .filter(([key, value]) => key !== 'phones' && key !== 'contactType' && value !== undefined)
    .map(([key]) => key);
  if (profile.phones.length > 0) names.push('phones');
  return names.sort();
}
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Replace `processRow` in full**

Delete the existing `processRow` (including the Task 11 temporary unwrap and its `TODO(ai-run-log)` comment) and write this. This is the complete function - no ellipses:

```ts
/**
 * Process ONE due row end-to-end, FILLING the caller's draft as it goes.
 *
 * NEVER THROWS on a known failure and never returns a bare discriminator: it
 * returns an outcome on every path - success, skip and failure alike - catching
 * internally at each stage and stamping draft.error.kind AT THE THROW SITE,
 * which is the only place that information exists (design 6.3/8).
 */
async function processRow(
  row: DueExtractionItem,
  nowIso: string,
  deps: ExtractionJobDeps,
  draft: RunDraft,
): Promise<ProcessRowResult> {
  const { repo, conversations, messages, contacts, driver, applyDeps, logger } = deps;
  const conversationId = row.conversationId;
  const cursor = row.cursor ?? '';

  const failed = (kind: RunErrorKind, err: unknown): ProcessRowResult => {
    draft.error = { kind, message: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) };
    return { record: true, outcome: 'failed' };
  };
  /**
   * complete() on ANY path, including a skip. A throw here is a FAILURE, never a
   * skip: complete() is what clears the claim, so a throw leaves the row CLAIMED
   * and out of byDueAt - and recording it as a skip means no repo.fail(), hence
   * no backoff, no attempt increment and no park (design 8). Returns a result to
   * return, or undefined to continue. draft.skipReason is set BEFORE this runs,
   * so a complete() failure still records WHICH skip was in progress.
   */
  const completeOrFail = async (nextCursor: string): Promise<ProcessRowResult | undefined> => {
    try {
      await repo.complete(conversationId, nextCursor, nowIso);
      return undefined;
    } catch (err) {
      return failed('complete', err);
    }
  };

  // --- claim ---------------------------------------------------------------
  // Claim BEFORE any work: the conditional `dueAt = listedDueAt` guard means a
  // row that slid forward (a newer inbound re-scheduled it) loses here, so a
  // debounce burst yields exactly one run at the final dueAt.
  const listedDueAt = row.dueAt;
  if (listedDueAt === undefined) return { record: false }; // defensive; listed rows carry it
  let claimed: boolean;
  try {
    claimed = await repo.claim(conversationId, nowIso, listedDueAt);
  } catch (err) {
    return failed('repo', err);
  }
  if (!claimed) {
    logger.debug({ conversationId }, 'extraction claim lost (slid or already claimed) - skipping');
    return { record: false };
  }

  // --- resolve the conversation + its 1:1 contact ---------------------------
  let conv: Awaited<ReturnType<typeof conversations.getById>>;
  let contact: ContactItem | undefined;
  try {
    conv = await conversations.getById(conversationId);
    const participant = conv?.participants?.[0];
    if (conv) {
      if (participant?.contactId) {
        contact = await contacts.getById(participant.contactId);
      } else {
        const phone = participant?.phone ?? conv.participant_phone;
        if (phone) contact = await contacts.findByPhone(phone);
      }
    }
  } catch (err) {
    // repo.claim, contacts.getById, contacts.findByPhone and
    // messages.listByConversation are all real, currently-unguarded throw sites
    // (design 6.3).
    return failed('repo', err);
  }

  // Nothing to extract for: a missing conversation/contact or a bare phone-ref
  // pointer item. NO window - this exits before the fetch (design 6.3).
  if (!conv || !contact || contact.contactId.startsWith(PHONE_REF_PREFIX)) {
    logger.debug({ conversationId }, 'extraction: nothing to extract (missing contact) - completing');
    draft.skipReason = 'no_contact';
    const failure = await completeOrFail(cursor);
    return failure ?? { record: true, outcome: 'skipped' };
  }
  if (contact.type === 'landlord' || contact.type === 'partner' || contact.type === 'team_member') {
    logger.debug({ conversationId, contactType: contact.type }, 'extraction: ineligible contact type - completing');
    draft.skipReason = 'ineligible_type';
    const failure = await completeOrFail(cursor);
    return failure ?? { record: true, outcome: 'skipped' };
  }
  draft.contactId = contact.contactId;

  // --- transcript window ----------------------------------------------------
  // Newest N messages, REVERSED to chronological, with rows older than
  // MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS dropped. The window deliberately INCLUDES messages
  // at/before the cursor for context - the cursor marks progress, not a hard
  // filter; we only RUN when a client utterance is newer than the cursor.
  let newestFirst: MessageItem[];
  try {
    newestFirst = await messages.listByConversation(conversationId, { limit: MAX_TRANSCRIPT_MESSAGES });
  } catch (err) {
    return failed('repo', err);
  }
  const cutoff = new Date(Date.parse(nowIso) - MAX_TRANSCRIPT_AGE_DAYS * DAY_MS).toISOString();
  const chronological = [...newestFirst].reverse();
  const fresh = chronological.filter((m) => m.created_at >= cutoff);
  const agedOutTsMsgIds = chronological.filter((m) => m.created_at < cutoff).map((m) => m.tsMsgId);
  const newestTsMsgId = fresh[fresh.length - 1]?.tsMsgId;

  // LIGHT window first (design 6.3, as amended): ids, cursor and the fetch cap,
  // with NO capping and NO hashing. That work happens only below the gates,
  // because `no_new_client` is the common steady-state outcome and doing it
  // purely to log it would put it on the hot path. A skip records THIS.
  draft.window = buildLightRunWindow({
    cursor,
    fetchedCount: newestFirst.length,
    agedOutTsMsgIds,
    messages: fresh.map((m) => ({ tsMsgId: m.tsMsgId, type: m.type, direction: m.direction })),
    ...(newestTsMsgId !== undefined && { newestTsMsgId }),
  });

  // Freshness gate. A voice- or triage-triggered run BYPASSES it entirely - both
  // are signals for content the cursor logic cannot see (a late transcript, or
  // newly-applicable tenant facts after a triage flip). On an SMS-triggered run,
  // a fresh COMPLETED-transcript call also counts as new client-side content.
  const hasNewClient =
    row.channel === 'voice' ||
    row.channel === 'triage' ||
    fresh.some(
      (m) =>
        m.tsMsgId > cursor &&
        (m.direction === 'inbound' || (m.type === 'call' && m.transcript_status === 'completed')),
    );
  if (!hasNewClient) {
    logger.debug({ conversationId }, 'extraction: no new client messages since cursor - completing');
    draft.skipReason = 'no_new_client';
    const failure = await completeOrFail(cursor);
    return failure ?? { record: true, outcome: 'skipped' };
  }

  // A voice-/triage-triggered run bypasses the gate above, so an empty window
  // would fall through and throw. Guard it explicitly.
  if (fresh.length === 0) {
    logger.debug({ conversationId }, 'extraction: empty transcript window - completing');
    draft.skipReason = 'empty_window';
    const failure = await completeOrFail(cursor);
    return failure ?? { record: true, outcome: 'skipped' };
  }

  // --- capping, budget, and the FULL window --------------------------------
  // Tiered per-message caps (see the constants' header comment): post-cursor
  // messages get their one generous full-fidelity read; already-extracted ones
  // stay as tightly-capped reconciliation context.
  const perMessage: WindowMessagePieces[] = fresh.map((m) => {
    const raw = toUtterances(m);
    const capChars = m.tsMsgId > cursor ? NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP : SEEN_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP;
    return {
      tsMsgId: m.tsMsgId,
      type: m.type,
      direction: m.direction,
      raw,
      capped: capUtterances(raw, capChars),
      capChars,
    };
  });
  // Whole-window budget: fill newest-first and STOP at the first overflow, so
  // the newest (unprocessed) content always wins and the window stays a
  // contiguous newest-N slice - the oldest context drops first. A capped message
  // is at most NEW_MESSAGE_CHAR_CAP < budget, so the newest always fits.
  const included = new Set<string>();
  let windowChars = 0;
  for (let k = perMessage.length - 1; k >= 0; k -= 1) {
    const size = perMessage[k]!.capped.reduce((n, u) => n + u.text.length, 0);
    if (windowChars + size > WINDOW_CHAR_BUDGET) break;
    windowChars += size;
    included.add(perMessage[k]!.tsMsgId);
  }
  const transcript = perMessage.filter((p) => included.has(p.tsMsgId)).flatMap((p) => p.capped);
  // Spec Layer 3: any unknown-speaker (Speaker N) call line demotes the whole run
  // to suggest-only in apply.
  const hasInferredRoleContent = transcript.some((u) => u.speaker === 'unknown');

  draft.window = buildFullRunWindow({
    cursor,
    fetchedCount: newestFirst.length,
    agedOutTsMsgIds,
    perMessage,
    included,
    hasInferredRoleContent,
    ...(newestTsMsgId !== undefined && { newestTsMsgId }),
  });

  // --- the model call -------------------------------------------------------
  const profile = toProfile(contact);
  draft.profileFieldsPopulated = profileFieldNames(profile);

  const call = await driver.extract({ transcript, profile });
  draft.driver = call.meta.driver;
  if (call.meta.model !== undefined) draft.model = call.meta.model;
  if (call.meta.promptFingerprint !== undefined) draft.promptFingerprint = call.meta.promptFingerprint;
  if (call.meta.usage !== undefined) draft.usage = call.meta.usage;
  if (call.meta.rawText !== undefined) draft.rawText = call.meta.rawText;

  const warnUnexplained = (target: DecisionTarget): void => {
    // Target NAME only - never a value. A reasonless drop means apply gained a
    // branch, or schema.ts gained a fold site, that nothing accounts for.
    logger.warn({ conversationId, target }, 'ai run log: unexplained dropped decision');
  };

  if (!call.ok) {
    // error.kind is set AT THE THROW SITE - the failure discriminator IS the
    // stage. A writer that cannot see which stage failed cannot distinguish
    // these, which is the whole reason ExtractionCall is discriminated (6.3/6.4).
    // A `failed` run STILL records window and decisions (6.3), so build them
    // BEFORE returning.
    draft.decisions = buildDecisions({
      ops: parseExtractionOps(call.meta.rawText),
      rawTextPresent: call.meta.rawText !== undefined,
      applyDecisions: [],
      onUnexplained: warnUnexplained,
    });
    draft.notedLines = 0;
    return failed(call.failure, new Error(call.message));
  }
  draft.rawResult = call.result;

  // applyExtraction is DELIBERATELY NOT wrapped: it guards every side effect and
  // does not throw, which is why there is no `apply` error kind (design 6.3). If
  // it ever does throw, runDueExtractions' backstop catches it as `repo`.
  const applyOutcome = await applyExtraction(applyDeps, {
    contact,
    conversationId,
    cursorTsMsgId: newestTsMsgId,
    result: call.result,
    hasInferredRoleContent,
    runId: draft.runId,
  });
  draft.decisions = buildDecisions({
    ops: parseExtractionOps(call.meta.rawText),
    rawTextPresent: call.meta.rawText !== undefined,
    applyDecisions: applyOutcome.decisions,
    onUnexplained: warnUnexplained,
  });
  draft.notedLines = applyOutcome.notedLines;
  draft.displaced = applyOutcome.displaced;

  // Keep the cursor MONOTONIC: a voice-triggered run's newestTsMsgId can be OLDER
  // than the current cursor (the call row predates a cursor an SMS run already
  // advanced), and complete() writes the cursor unconditionally - advancing to
  // max(newest, cursor) prevents a regression that would make a later SMS run
  // re-examine already-processed messages.
  const nextCursor = newestTsMsgId !== undefined && newestTsMsgId > cursor ? newestTsMsgId : cursor;
  const completeFailure = await completeOrFail(nextCursor);
  if (completeFailure) {
    // The model ran and the contact was mutated. The record must NOT be erased
    // by a complete() failure (design 8).
    return completeFailure;
  }
  logger.info({ conversationId }, 'extraction run complete');

  // applied = at least one decision reached `wrote` or `suggested`, OR
  // notedLines > 0. A run full of drops is a no_op (design section 6).
  const touched =
    applyOutcome.wrote.length > 0 || applyOutcome.suggested.length > 0 || applyOutcome.notedLines > 0;
  return { record: true, outcome: touched ? 'applied' : 'no_op' };
}
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Add `recordRun` above `runDueExtractions`**

```ts
/**
 * The SINGLE envelope write, on every terminal path. STRICTLY BEST-EFFORT:
 * wrapped, logged, swallowed. It must never fail an extraction run, re-arm a due
 * row, or burn a retry attempt - observability that can break what it observes is
 * worse than none (design 8).
 *
 * Deliberately NOT idempotent: if complete() fails and the row re-arms, the retry
 * writes a SECOND run record. The model really was called twice.
 */
async function recordRun(deps: ExtractionJobDeps, outcome: RunOutcome, draft: RunDraft): Promise<void> {
  try {
    const finishedAt = deps.now();
    const durationMs = Math.max(0, Date.parse(finishedAt) - Date.parse(draft.startedAt));
    const record: AiRunRecordInput = {
      runId: draft.runId,
      startedAt: draft.startedAt,
      finishedAt,
      durationMs,
      conversationId: draft.conversationId,
      ...(draft.contactId !== undefined && { contactId: draft.contactId }),
      trigger: draft.trigger,
      outcome,
      ...(draft.skipReason !== undefined && { skipReason: draft.skipReason }),
      ...(draft.error !== undefined && {
        error: {
          kind: draft.error.kind,
          message: draft.error.message,
          attempts: draft.error.attempts ?? 0,
          parked: draft.error.parked ?? false,
        },
      }),
      // A run that exited before the driver was consulted still has to name one;
      // the configured driver is the honest answer.
      driver: draft.driver ?? deps.driver.kind,
      ...(draft.model !== undefined && { model: draft.model }),
      ...(draft.promptFingerprint !== undefined && { promptFingerprint: draft.promptFingerprint }),
      ...(draft.usage !== undefined && { usage: draft.usage }),
      ...(draft.window !== undefined && { window: draft.window }),
      ...(draft.profileFieldsPopulated !== undefined && {
        profileFieldsPopulated: draft.profileFieldsPopulated,
      }),
      ...(draft.rawText !== undefined && { rawText: draft.rawText }),
      ...(draft.rawResult !== undefined && { rawResult: draft.rawResult }),
      decisions: draft.decisions ?? {},
      notedLines: draft.notedLines ?? 0,
    };
    await deps.aiRuns.putRun(record);
  } catch (err) {
    // Ids only - NEVER the draft. A run whose record fails to write is invisible
    // while the extraction itself succeeded: the accepted cost of best-effort
    // recording (design 14). It logs, which is what makes it findable.
    deps.logger.warn(
      { conversationId: draft.conversationId, runId: draft.runId, err },
      'ai run log write failed (best-effort - extraction unaffected)',
    );
  }
}
```

- [ ] **Step 2: Rewrite the `for` body in `runDueExtractions`**

```ts
  for (const row of dueRows) {
    // The draft is allocated HERE, before processRow runs, so the backstop below
    // can still write whatever was built - including any runId already stamped
    // onto suggestion rows by applyExtraction (design 8).
    const draft = newRunDraft(row, deps.now());
    let result: ProcessRowResult;
    try {
      result = await processRow(row, nowIso, deps, draft);
    } catch (err) {
      // BACKSTOP - per-row batch isolation. processRow not throwing on KNOWN
      // failures does not mean it can never throw: an unhandled defect, an OOM,
      // or a repo path nobody enumerated would otherwise abort the ENTIRE poll
      // batch. The returned outcome is the primary path; this is the safety net,
      // and it must still call repo.fail (design 8, F1). It records the SAME
      // draft, so nothing built before the throw is lost.
      if (draft.error === undefined) {
        draft.error = { kind: 'repo', message: err instanceof Error ? err.message : String(err) };
      }
      result = { record: true, outcome: 'failed' };
    }
    if (!result.record) continue; // claim lost / defensive - deliberately unrecorded

    const { outcome } = result;
    if (outcome === 'applied' || outcome === 'no_op') processed += 1;

    if (outcome === 'failed') {
      failed += 1;
      // Re-arm with exponential backoff off the CURRENT attempt count, or PARK
      // once the next attempt would reach MAX_EXTRACTION_ATTEMPTS. Isolated per
      // row. UNCHANGED from the pre-run-log behavior.
      const attempts = row.attempts ?? 0;
      const parked = attempts + 1 >= MAX_EXTRACTION_ATTEMPTS;
      const nextDueAt = parked
        ? null
        : new Date(
            Date.parse(nowIso) + Math.min(config.aiExtractionDebounceMs * 2 ** attempts, MAX_BACKOFF_MS),
          ).toISOString();
      // Ids/counts/flags only - NEVER the draft (it carries PII by design).
      logger.error({ conversationId: row.conversationId, attempts, parked }, 'extraction poll: row failed');
      if (draft.error !== undefined) draft.error = { ...draft.error, attempts, parked };
      try {
        await repo.fail(row.conversationId, draft.error?.message ?? 'unknown', nextDueAt);
      } catch (failErr) {
        logger.error({ conversationId: row.conversationId, err: failErr }, 'extraction poll: fail() write errored');
      }
    }

    await recordRun(deps, outcome, draft);
    await stampSuperseded(deps, draft); // Task 23
  }
```
